\|\bm{G}(\lambda,t)-\bm{G}^{\perp}(\lambda,t)\|\lesssim\frac{t\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{rn}\log n

holds simultaneously for all
1/\sqrt{n}\leq t\leq 1
and
\lambda

with
|\lambda|\in[2|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|/3,\,4|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|/3]
.
Towards this end, we shall resort to the epsilon-net argument once
again. Choose
\epsilon_{1}=c/\sqrt{n}
for some sufficiently small
constant
c>0
, and let
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon_{1}}
be an
\epsilon
-net
for
[1/\sqrt{n},1]
such that (1) it has cardinality
|\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon_{1}}|\lesssim\sqrt{n}
;
(2) for any
t\in[1/\sqrt{n},1]
, there exists some point
\widehat{t}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon_{1}}

obeying
|\widehat{t}-t|\leq\epsilon_{1}
.

•

Applying Lemma
1
with the noise matrix
chosen as
t\bm{H}
and applying the union bound, we see that with
probability exceeding
1-O\left(n^{-11}\right)
, one has

\displaystyle\sup_{\lambda:\,|\lambda|\in[2|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|/3,\,4|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|/3]}\|\bm{G}(\lambda,\widehat{t})-\bm{G}^{\perp}(\lambda,\widehat{t})\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\widehat{t}^{2}\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\big(\sqrt{rn\log n}+r\log n\big)\leq\frac{\widehat{t}\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{rn}\log n

simultaneously for all
\widehat{t}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon_{1}}
,
where in the second line we have used
\widehat{t}^{2}\leq\widehat{t}

since
\widehat{t}\in[0,1]
.

•

For any
t\in[1/\sqrt{n},1]
, let
\widehat{t}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon_{1}}

be a point obeying
|\widehat{t}-t|\leq\epsilon_{1}
. Recognizing
that
\bm{G}(\lambda,\widehat{t})-\bm{G}(\lambda,t)\overset{\mathrm{d}}{=}t^{2}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{ur}}\big(\lambda\bm{I}_{n-r}-t\bm{H}_{\mathsf{lr}}\big)^{-1}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{ur}}^{\top}-\widehat{t}^{2}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{ur}}\big(\lambda\bm{I}_{n-r}-\widehat{t}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{lr}}\big)^{-1}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{ur}}^{\top}
,
one can bound

\displaystyle\|\bm{G}(\lambda,\widehat{t})-\bm{G}(\lambda,t)\|\leq\big\|\widehat{t}^{2}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{ur}}\big(\lambda\bm{I}_{n-r}-\widehat{t}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{lr}}\big)^{-1}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{ur}}^{\top}-t^{2}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{ur}}\big(\lambda\bm{I}_{n-r}-t\bm{H}_{\mathsf{lr}}\big)^{-1}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{ur}}^{\top}\big\|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\big\|\widehat{t}^{2}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{ur}}\big(\lambda\bm{I}_{n-r}-\widehat{t}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{lr}}\big)^{-1}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{ur}}^{\top}-t^{2}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{ur}}\big(\lambda\bm{I}_{n-r}-\widehat{t}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{lr}}\big)^{-1}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{ur}}^{\top}\big\|

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+\big\|t^{2}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{ur}}\big(\lambda\bm{I}_{n-r}-\widehat{t}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{lr}}\big)^{-1}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{ur}}^{\top}-t^{2}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{ur}}\big(\lambda\bm{I}_{n-r}-t\bm{H}_{\mathsf{lr}}\big)^{-1}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{ur}}^{\top}\big\|

\displaystyle\quad\leq|t-\widehat{t}|\cdot|t+\widehat{t}|\cdot\|\bm{H}_{\mathsf{ur}}\|^{2}\big\|\big(\lambda\bm{I}_{n-r}-\widehat{t}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{lr}}\big)^{-1}\big\|+t^{2}\|\bm{H}_{\mathsf{ur}}\|^{2}\big\|\big(\lambda\bm{I}_{n-r}-t\bm{H}_{\mathsf{lr}}\big)^{-1}-\big(\lambda\bm{I}_{n-r}-\widehat{t}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{lr}}\big)^{-1}\big\|.

Recalling the notation that
\gamma_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\gamma_{n-r}

represent the eigenvalues of
\bm{H}_{\mathsf{lr}}
, we have

\big\|\big(\lambda\bm{I}_{n-r}-\widehat{t}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{lr}}\big)^{-1}\big\|=\max_{i}\Big|\frac{1}{\lambda-\widehat{t}\gamma_{i}}\Big|\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\qquad\text{and}

\displaystyle\big\|\big(\lambda\bm{I}_{n-r}-t\bm{H}_{\mathsf{lr}}\big)^{-1}-\big(\lambda\bm{I}_{n-r}-\widehat{t}\bm{H}_{\mathsf{lr}}\big)^{-1}\big\|

\displaystyle=\max_{i}\Big|\frac{1}{\lambda-t\gamma_{i}}-\frac{1}{\lambda-\widehat{t}\gamma_{i}}\Big|=\max_{i}\Big|\frac{(t-\widehat{t})\gamma_{i}}{(\lambda-t\gamma_{i})(\lambda-\widehat{t}\gamma_{i})}\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|t-\widehat{t}|}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\leq\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}},

where we have used the bounds
2\,|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|/3\leq|\lambda|\leq 4\,|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|/3
,

|\gamma_{i}|\leq\lambda_{\min}^{\star}/3
and
\widehat{t}\leq t+\epsilon_{1}\leq 1.1
.
Combining these with the high-probability bound
\|\bm{H}_{\mathsf{ur}}\|\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{n}
,
we arrive at

\|\bm{G}(\lambda,\widehat{t})-\bm{G}(\lambda,t)\|\lesssim\epsilon_{1}\cdot t\cdot\sigma^{2}n\cdot\frac{1}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}+t^{2}\cdot\sigma^{2}n\cdot\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\lesssim\frac{t\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{n},

where the last step arises since
t^{2}\leq t
for any
t\in[0,1]
.
Similarly, this bound holds for
\|\bm{G}^{\perp}(\lambda,\widehat{t})-\bm{G}^{\perp}(\lambda,t)\|

as well.

Putting these two upper bounds together, we conclude that with probability
at least
1-O(n^{-11})
,

\displaystyle\|\bm{G}(\lambda,t)-\bm{G}^{\perp}(\lambda,t)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{G}(\lambda,\widehat{t})-\bm{G}^{\perp}(\lambda,\widehat{t})\|+\|\bm{G}(\lambda,\widehat{t})-\bm{G}(\lambda,t)\|+\|\bm{G}^{\perp}(\lambda,\widehat{t})-\bm{G}^{\perp}(\lambda,t)\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\widehat{t}\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{rn}\log n+\frac{t\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{n}\asymp\frac{t\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{rn}\log n

holds simultaneously for all
\lambda
with
|\lambda|\in[2|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|/3,\,4|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|/3]

and all
t\in[1/\sqrt{n},1]
. Finally, taking a union bound over

1\leq l\leq r
concludes the proof.

Appendix C
Proofs of auxiliary lemmas for Theorem
1

C.1
Proof of Lemma
3

To begin with, let us first analyze the eigenvalues of
\bm{M}^{(l)}
,
which is accomplished by the following lemma.

Lemma 13
.

Instate the assumptions
of Theorem
1
. With probability at
least
1-O(n^{-10}),
one has

\displaystyle\big|\lambda_{k}^{(l)}-\gamma(\lambda_{k}^{(l)})-\lambda_{k}^{\star}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}},\qquad 1\leq k<l,

(C.1a)

\displaystyle\big|\lambda_{k}^{(l)}-\gamma(\lambda_{k}^{(l)})-\lambda_{k+1}^{\star}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}},\qquad l\leq k<r,

(C.1b)

\displaystyle\big|\lambda_{k}^{(l)}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{H}\|\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{n},\qquad k\geq r,

(C.1c)

where
\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}}=C_{1}\sigma\sqrt{r}\log n

for some sufficiently large constant
C_{1}>0
and
\gamma(\cdot)

is defined in (
5.45
).

Proof.

See
Appendix
C.1.1
.∎

Lemma
13
can then be invoked to study
Lemma
3
. Recalling the fact

\displaystyle\lambda_{k}-\gamma(\lambda_{k})

\displaystyle\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}}}(\lambda_{k}^{\star}),\qquad\qquad 1\leq k\leq r

(C.2)

\displaystyle|\lambda_{k}|

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{H}\|\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{n},\qquad k>r

as shown in Theorem
7
, we are positioned
to prove the claim (
5.33
) as follows.

•

For any
\lambda
such that
|\lambda|\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{n}
, one
has

\displaystyle\big|\lambda_{l}-\lambda\big|

\displaystyle\geq\big|\lambda_{l}-\gamma(\lambda_{l})\big|-\big|\gamma(\lambda_{l})\big|-|\lambda|\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{\geq}\big|\lambda_{l}^{\star}\big|-\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}}-\big|\gamma(\lambda_{l})\big|-O(\sigma\sqrt{n})

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\geq}\big|\lambda_{l}^{\star}\big|-O(\sigma\sqrt{r}\log n)-O\Big(\frac{\sigma^{2}n}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\Big)-O(\sigma\sqrt{n})\overset{(\mathrm{iii})}{\gtrsim}\big|\lambda_{l}^{\star}\big|,

where (i) arises from (
C.2
) and (
C.1c
),
(ii) follows since

\big|\gamma(\lambda_{l})\big|=\Bigg|\sum_{i}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{i}\big((\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{U}^{\star\perp}\big)}\Bigg|\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}n}{|\lambda_{l}|-\|\bm{H}\|}\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}n}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}},

(C.3)

and (iii) is valid as long as
\sigma\sqrt{r}\log n\leq c_{0}\lambda_{\min}^{\star}

and
\sigma\sqrt{n}\leq c_{0}\lambda_{\min}^{\star}
hold for some
small constant
c_{0}>0
.

•

For any
\lambda
satisfying
\lambda-\gamma(\lambda)\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}}}(\lambda_{k}^{\star})

for some
1\leq k\leq r
, we define an auxiliary function
f:\pm[2\lambda_{\min}^{\star}/3,\,4\lambda_{\max}^{\star}/3]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}

by

f(\lambda):=\lambda-\gamma(\lambda),

(C.4)

where we denote
\pm[a,b]=[-b,-a]\cup[a,b]
for
a<b
and
\gamma(\cdot)

is defined in (
5.45
). To begin
with, for any
\lambda
with
|\lambda|\in[\lambda_{\min}^{\star}/3,\,2\lambda_{\max}^{\star}]

one has

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=1+\sum_{i}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\big[\lambda-\lambda_{i}\big((\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{U}^{\star\perp}\big)\big]^{2}}\geq 1-\frac{\sigma^{2}n}{(\tfrac{1}{3}\lambda_{\min}-\|\bm{H}\|)^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{2},

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\leq 1+\frac{\sigma^{2}n}{(\tfrac{1}{3}\lambda_{\min}-\|\bm{H}\|)^{2}}\leq\frac{3}{2},

with the proviso that
\sigma\sqrt{n}\leq c_{0}\lambda_{\min}^{\star}

for some constant
c_{0}>0
small enough. This means that within
the range
|\lambda|\in[2\lambda_{\min}^{\star}/3,\,4\lambda_{\max}^{\star}/3]
,
the function
f(\cdot)
is monotonically increasing and continuous.
As a result, the inverse of
f(\cdot)
exists, which is also monotonically
increasing and obeys

\frac{2}{3}\leq\frac{\mathrm{d}f^{-1}(\tau)}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\leq 2\qquad\forall\tau\text{ with }|\tau|\in[\lambda_{\min}^{\star}/2,3\lambda_{\max}^{\star}/2].

(C.5)

In view of (
5.23
) and the condition that

\lambda-\gamma(\lambda)\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}}}(\lambda_{k}^{\star})

for some
1\leq k\leq r
, one can invoke (
C.5
)
to reach

\displaystyle\big|\lambda_{l}-\lambda\big|

\displaystyle=\big|f^{-1}(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+O(\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}}))-f^{-1}(\lambda_{k}^{\star}+O(\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}}))\big|

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\tau:\,|\tau|\in[\lambda_{\min}^{\star}/2,\,3\lambda_{\max}^{\star}/2]}\Big|\frac{\mathrm{d}f^{-1}(\tau)}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\Big|\big|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{k}^{\star}+O(\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}})\big|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2}{3}\,\big|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{k}^{\star}+O(\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}})\big|\gtrsim\big|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{k}^{\star}\big|,

where the last inequality holds due to our eigen-gap assumption (
3.3
)
and the fact that
\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}}\asymp\sigma\sqrt{r}\log n
.

•

With the analysis above, we note that (
5.33
)
is an immediate consequence of (
C.1
) in Lemma
13
.

C.1.1
Proof of Lemma
13

The proof of this lemma follows from the same argument employed to
establish Theorem
7
. The idea is to
invoke Theorem
6
to analyze the spectrum
of
\bm{M}^{(l)}
. Before proceeding, we introduce several notation
tailored to this setting as well as a few simple facts. To begin with,
we define

\displaystyle\bm{M}^{\star(l)}

\displaystyle:=(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{M}^{\star}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}=(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{\Lambda}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}=\bm{U}^{\star(l)}\bm{\Lambda}^{\star(l)}\bm{U}^{\star(l)\top},

\displaystyle\bm{H}^{(l)}

\displaystyle:=(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp},

where we recall the definitions of
\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}
(resp.
\bm{U}^{\star(l)}

and
\bm{\Lambda}^{\star(l)}
) in (
5.13
) (resp. (
5.32
)).
In addition, denote

\displaystyle\bm{G}^{(l)}(\lambda)

\displaystyle:=\bm{U}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{H}^{(l)}\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}\big(\lambda\bm{I}_{n-r}-(\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}^{(l)}\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}\big)^{-1}(\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}^{(l)}\bm{U}^{\star(l)},

\displaystyle\bm{G}^{(l)\perp}(\lambda)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{G}^{(l)}(\lambda)\mid(\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}^{(l)}\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}\big],

where
\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}
is defined in (
5.32
)
and the expectation is taken assuming that
\lambda
is independent
of
\bm{H}
. By construction, one has
\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}=\bm{U}^{\star\perp}
,
and consequently

\displaystyle(\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}^{(l)}\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}

\displaystyle=(\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp})^{\top}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}=(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{U}^{\star\perp},

\displaystyle(\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}^{(l)}\bm{U}^{\star(l)}

\displaystyle=(\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp})^{\top}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}\bm{U}^{\star(l)}=(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{P}^{(l)},

(C.6)

where
\bm{P}^{(l)}
is obtained by removing the
l
-th column of

\bm{I}_{r}
, namely,

\bm{P}^{(l)}:=\begin{bmatrix}\bm{I}_{l-1}&\bm{0}\\
0&0\\
\bm{0}&\bm{I}_{r-l}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times(r-1)}.

Therefore,
\bm{G}^{(l)}(\lambda)
and
\bm{G}^{(l)\perp}(\lambda)

admit the following simplified expressions

\displaystyle\bm{G}^{(l)}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\bm{U}^{\star\top}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{U}^{\star\perp}\big(\lambda\bm{I}_{n-r}-(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{U}^{\star\perp}\big)^{-1}(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{U}^{\star}

\displaystyle=\bm{P}^{(l)\top}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\bm{H}\bm{U}^{\star\perp}\big(\lambda\bm{I}_{n-r}-(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{U}^{\star\perp}\big)^{-1}(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{P}^{(l)}

\displaystyle=\bm{P}^{(l)\top}\bm{G}(\lambda)\bm{P}^{(l)}

\displaystyle\bm{G}^{(l)\perp}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{G}^{(l)}(\lambda)\mid(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{U}^{\star\perp}\big],

where
\bm{G}(\lambda)
is defined in (
5.14b
).

With the above preparation in place, we can repeat the proof of Theorem

7
to obtain

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{U}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{H}^{(l)}\bm{U}^{\star(l)}\big\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma\big(\sqrt{r}+\sqrt{\log n}\big)

\displaystyle\sup_{\lambda:\,|\lambda|\in[2\lambda_{\min}^{\star}/3,\,4\lambda_{\max}^{\star}/3]}\big\|\bm{G}^{(l)}(\lambda)-\bm{G}^{(l)\perp}(\lambda)\big\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\big(\sqrt{rn\log n}+r\log n\big)

\displaystyle\bm{G}^{(l)\perp}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\gamma(\lambda)\bm{P}^{(l)\top}\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\bm{P}^{(l)}

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10}),
where
\gamma(\cdot)

is defined in (
5.45
). The above
observations reveal that the
k
-th eigenvalue of
\bm{M}^{\star(l)}+\bm{G}^{(l)\perp}(\lambda)

is given by

\lambda_{k}\big(\bm{M}^{\star(l)}+\bm{G}^{(l)\perp}(\lambda)\big)=\begin{cases}\lambda_{k}^{\star}+\gamma(\lambda),&1\leq k\leq l-1;\\
\lambda_{k+1}^{\star}+\gamma(\lambda),&l\leq k\leq r-1;\\
0,&r\leq k.\end{cases}

As a result, repeating the same arguments of Theorem
7

(which we omit for brevity) immediately establishes the claim of this
lemma.

C.2
Proof of Lemma
4

Let
\bm{U}^{(l)}\bm{\Lambda}^{(l)}\bm{U}^{(l)\top}
represent the
eigen-decomposition of
\bm{M}^{(l)}
, where
\bm{U}^{(l)}=\big[\bm{u}_{1}^{(l)},\cdots,\bm{u}_{n-1}^{(l)}\big]
.
We can derive

\displaystyle\big\|\big(\lambda_{l}\bm{I}_{n-1}-\bm{M}^{(l)}\big)^{-1}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\big\|\bm{U}^{(l)}\big(\lambda_{l}\bm{I}_{n-1}-\bm{\Lambda}^{(l)}\big)^{-1}\bm{U}^{(l)\top}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\big\|\big(\lambda_{l}\bm{I}_{n-1}-\bm{\Lambda}^{(l)}\big)^{-1}\bm{U}^{(l)\top}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq k<n}\bigg(\frac{\bm{u}_{k}^{(l)\top}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}}{\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k}^{(l)}}\bigg)^{2}.

(C.7)

By construction (cf. (
5.29
)), the matrix

\bm{M}^{(l)}
(and hence
\bm{\Lambda}^{(l)}
and
\bm{U}^{(l)}
)
is independent of
(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}
,
thus indicating that

\bm{U}^{(l)\top}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n-1}).

In addition, notice that the distribution of
\bm{U}^{(l)\top}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}

is independent with
\bm{U}^{(l)}
and
\bm{\Lambda}^{(l)}
. In
what follows, we shall look at (
C.7
) by controlling the
sum over
k<r
and the sum over
k\geq r
separately.

•

To begin with, let us upper bound
\sum_{1\leq k<r}\Big(\frac{\bm{u}_{k}^{(l)\top}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}}{\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k}^{(l)}}\Big)^{2}
.
Given a sequence of i.i.d. standard Gaussian random variables
Z_{i}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
,
one knows from the standard Gaussian concentration inequality that
the following holds with probability at least
1-O(n^{-20})
:

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i\leq n}|Z_{i}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{\log n};

(C.8a)

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i\leq n}|Z_{i}^{2}-\sigma^{2}|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq n}|Z_{i}-\sigma|\cdot\max_{1\leq i\leq n}|Z_{i}+\sigma|\lesssim\sigma^{2}\log n.

(C.8b)

In addition, Lemma
3

tells us that
\min_{1\leq k<r}\big|\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k}^{(l)}\big|^{2}\gtrsim\min_{i:i\neq l}|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{i}^{\star}|^{2}
.
These two bounds taken together give

\sum_{1\leq k<r}\bigg(\frac{\bm{u}_{k}^{(l)\top}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}}{\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k}^{(l)}}\bigg)^{2}\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}r\log n}{\min_{i:i\neq l}|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{i}^{\star}|^{2}}=\frac{\sigma^{2}r\log n}{\big(\Delta_{l}^{\star}\big)^{2}}.

(C.9)

•

Next, we move on to the remaining term (the sum over
r\leq k<n
).
We claim for the moment that: with probability exceeding
1-O(n^{-10})
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{r\leq k<n}\bigg(\frac{\bm{u}_{k}^{(l)\top}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}}{\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k}^{(l)}}\bigg)^{2}-\sum_{r\leq k<n}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{(\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k}^{(l)})^{2}}\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}\sqrt{n\log n},

(C.10)

The proof of this claim is deferred to the end of this section. It
then suffices to control the term
\sum_{r\leq k<n}\sigma^{2}/(\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k}^{(l)})^{2}
,
which is established in the lemma below (with the proof postponed
to Appendix
C.2.1
).

Lemma 14
.

Instate the assumptions
of Theorem
1
. With probability at
least
1-O(n^{-10})
,

\bigg|\sum_{r\leq k\leq n-1}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k}^{(l)})^{2}}-\sum_{r+1\leq k\leq n}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k})^{2}}\bigg|\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}.

(C.11)

As a consequence, we have

\bigg|\sum_{r\leq k\leq n-1}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k}^{(l)})^{2}}\bigg|\vee\bigg|\sum_{r+1\leq k\leq n}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k})^{2}}\bigg|\lesssim\frac{n}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}.

(C.12)

Therefore, combining (
C.10
) and (
C.11
)
gives

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{r\leq k\leq n-1}\bigg(\frac{\bm{u}_{k}^{(l)\top}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}}{\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k}^{(l)}}\bigg)^{2}-\sum_{r+1\leq k\leq n}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{(\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k})^{2}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\bigg|\sum_{r\leq k\leq n-1}\bigg(\frac{\bm{u}_{k}^{(l)\top}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}}{\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k}^{(l)}}\bigg)^{2}-\sum_{r\leq k<n}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{(\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k}^{(l)})^{2}}\bigg|+\bigg|\sum_{r\leq k<n}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{(\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k}^{(l)})^{2}}-\sum_{r+1\leq k\leq n}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{(\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k})^{2}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}\sqrt{n\log n}+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}\asymp\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}\sqrt{n\log n}.

(C.13)

•

Inserting (
C.9
) and (
C.13
) into
(
C.7
), we arrive at the advertised bound:

\displaystyle\big\|\big(\lambda_{l}\bm{I}_{n-1}-\bm{M}^{(l)}\big)^{-1}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{r+1\leq k\leq n}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{(\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k})^{2}}+O\bigg(\frac{\sigma^{2}r\log n}{\big(\Delta_{l}^{\star}\big)^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}\sqrt{n\log n}\bigg)

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{\sigma^{2}n}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}+O\bigg(\frac{\sigma^{2}r\log n}{\big(\Delta_{l}^{\star}\big)^{2}}\bigg)\ll 1,

where the last inequality holds due to our noise assumption (
3.3
).

Proof of the claim (
C.10
).

Note that
\bm{u}_{k}^{(l)\top}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}\overset{\mathsf{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})

is independent of
\bm{\Lambda}^{(l)}
but depends on
\bm{\Lambda}
.
Therefore, we shall use the epsilon-net argument (i.e. Lemma
20
)
to bound it. Before proceeding, observe that from (
5.33
),
(
C.3
) and the condition
\sigma\sqrt{n}\ll\lambda_{\min}^{\star}
,
the following holds for any
\lambda
obeying
\lambda-\gamma(\lambda)\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}}}(\lambda_{l}^{\star})
:

|\lambda-\lambda_{k}^{(l)}|\geq|\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k}^{(l)}|-|\lambda-\lambda_{l}-\gamma(\lambda_{l})|-|\gamma(\lambda_{l})|\gtrsim|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|,\qquad k\geq r.

(C.14)

Now we begin to check the conditions of Lemma
20
.
Since
|f(x)-f(y)|\leq\sup_{x}|f^{\prime}(x)||x-y|
, the following holds
with probability at least
1-O(n^{-20})
for all
\lambda
with

\lambda-\gamma(\lambda)\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}}}(\lambda_{l}^{\star})
:

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\lambda}\sum_{r\leq k<n}\frac{\big(\bm{u}_{k}^{(l)\top}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}\big)^{2}-\sigma^{2}}{(\lambda-\lambda_{k}^{(l)})^{2}}\bigg|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\sum_{r\leq k<n}\frac{\big(\bm{u}_{k}^{(l)\top}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}\big)^{2}-\sigma^{2}}{(\lambda-\lambda_{k}^{(l)})^{3}}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq n\cdot\max_{r\leq k<n}\frac{1}{\big|\lambda-\lambda_{k}^{(l)}\big|^{3}}\cdot\max_{r\leq k<n}\big|\bm{u}_{k}^{(l)\top}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}\big)^{2}-\sigma^{2}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}n\log n}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 3}},

where we use (
C.8b
) and (
C.14
).
In addition, for any fixed
\lambda
obeying
\lambda-\gamma(\lambda)\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}}}(\lambda_{l}^{\star})
,
one has

\displaystyle\max_{r\leq k<n}\frac{1}{(\lambda-\lambda_{k}^{(l)})^{2}}\big\|\big(\bm{u}_{k}^{(l)\top}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}\big)^{2}-\sigma^{2}\big\|_{\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}=:L;

\displaystyle\sum_{r\leq k<n}\frac{1}{(\lambda-\lambda_{k}^{(l)})^{4}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\big(\bm{u}_{k}^{(l)\top}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}\big)^{2}-\sigma^{2}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\Big]\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{4}n}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 4}}=:V

where
\|\cdot\|_{\psi_{1}}
denote the sub-exponential norm. We
can then apply the matrix Bernstein inequality
(
Koltchinskii 2011
, Corollary 2.1)

to find: with probability exceeding
1-O(n^{-20})
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{r\leq k<n}\frac{\big(\bm{u}_{k}^{(l)\top}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}\big)^{2}-\sigma^{2}}{(\lambda-\lambda_{k}^{(l)})^{2}}

\displaystyle\bigg|\lesssim L\log^{2}n+\sqrt{V\log n}\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{n\log n}}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}.

Recognizing the fact that
\{\lambda\colon\lambda-\gamma(\lambda)\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}}}(\lambda_{l}^{\star})\}\subset\big[\lambda_{l}^{\star}-|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|/3,\,\lambda_{l}^{\star}+|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|/3\big]
,
the claim (
C.14
) immediately follows from
Lemma
20
.

C.2.1
Proof of Lemma
14

Let us look at (
C.12
) first. According to
Lemma
3
, we have
|\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k}^{(l)}|\gtrsim|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|

for all
k\geq r
, thus leading to

\bigg|\sum_{r\leq k\leq n-1}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k}^{(l)})^{2}}\bigg|\lesssim\frac{n}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}.

In addition, the upper bound for
\sum_{r\leq k\leq n-1}1/(\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k})^{2}

is an immediate consequence of (
C.11
). Therefore,
the remainder of the proof amounts to establishing (
C.11
),
which requires us to characterize the relation between the spectrums
of
\bm{M}^{(l)}=(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{M}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}

and
\bm{M}
.

Without loss of generality, assume that
\lambda_{l}^{\star}>0
,
and that there are
m
(resp.
r-m
) eigenvalues of
\bm{M}^{\star}

larger (resp. smaller) than
0
. By Weyl’s inequality (similar to
(
5.10
) in the proof of Theorem
7
),
it is easily seen that there are
m
eigenvalues of
\bm{M}
larger
than
c\sigma\sqrt{n}
and that there are
r-m
eigenvalues of
\bm{M}

smaller than
-c\sigma\sqrt{n}
, where
c>0
is some constant. Recalling
that
\{\lambda_{k}\}_{k=1}^{n}
are defined as the eigenvalues of

\bm{M}
satisfying
|\lambda_{1}|\geq\cdots\geq|\lambda_{n}|
,
we further denote by
\{\phi_{k}\}_{k=1}^{n}
the eigenvalues of

\bm{M}
so that
\phi_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\phi_{n}
. Consider the set
of eigenvalues of
\bm{M}
with magnitudes upper bounded by
c\sigma\sqrt{n}
.
We have the following relation:

\sum_{k:\,r+1\leq k\leq n}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k})^{2}}=\sum_{k:\,m+1\leq k\leq n-r+m}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\phi_{k})^{2}}.

(C.15)

Similarly, for
\bm{M}^{(l)}
we can write

\sum_{k:\,r\leq k\leq n-1}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k}^{(l)})^{2}}=\sum_{k:\,m\leq k\leq n-r+m-1}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\phi_{k}^{(l)})^{2}},

(C.16)

where
\{\phi_{k}^{(l)}\}_{k=1}^{n-1}
denote the eigenvalues of

\bm{M}^{(l)}
in descending order. As a result, in order to establish
(
C.11
), it is sufficient to show

\Big|\sum_{k:\,m\leq k\leq n-r+m-1}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\phi_{k}^{(l)})^{2}}-\sum_{k:\,m+1\leq k\leq n-r+m}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\phi_{k})^{2}}\Big|\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}.

(C.17)

In view of an eigenvalue interlacing result stated in Lemma
21
,
the definition
\bm{M}^{(l)}:=(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{M}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}

allows us to deduce that

\phi_{k+1}\leq\phi_{k}^{(l)}\leq\phi_{k}\qquad 1\leq k<n.

(C.18)

By the assumption
\lambda_{l}^{\star}>0
, one has
\lambda_{l}\geq\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\|\bm{H}\|\gtrsim\lambda_{l}^{\star}

and thus
\phi_{k}\leq\lambda_{l}
for all
k\geq m
. Consequently,
we know from (
C.18
) that for all
k\geq m
,

\phi_{k+1}\leq\phi_{k}^{(l)}\leq\phi_{k}\leq\lambda_{l},

which further implies that

\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\phi_{k+1})^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\phi_{k}^{(l)})^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\phi_{k})^{2}},\qquad k\geq m.

This enables us to bound

\displaystyle\sum_{k:\,m\leq k\leq n-r+m-1}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\phi_{k}^{(l)})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\phi_{m}^{(l)})^{2}}+\sum_{k:\,m+1\leq k\leq n-r+m-1}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\phi_{k})^{2}};

\displaystyle\sum_{k:\,m\leq k\leq n-r+m-1}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\phi_{k}^{(l)})^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{k:\,m\leq k\leq n-r+m-1}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\phi_{k+1})^{2}}=\sum_{k:\,m+1\leq k\leq n-r+m}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\phi_{k})^{2}}.

Consequently, we conclude that

\Big|\sum_{k:\,m\leq k\leq n-r+m-1}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\phi_{k}^{(l)})^{2}}-\sum_{k:\,m+1\leq k\leq n-r+m}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\phi_{k})^{2}}\Big|\leq\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\phi_{m}^{(l)})^{2}}\asymp\frac{1}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}},

where the last relation holds since
|\phi_{m}^{(l)}|\leq\|\bm{H}\|

and hence
\lambda_{l}-\phi_{m}^{(l)}\asymp\lambda_{l}\asymp\lambda_{l}^{\star}
.

The above analysis can be easily adopted to handle the case where

\lambda_{l}^{\star}<0
as well (which we omit here for brevity).
Therefore, we have finished the proof.

C.3
Proof of Lemma
5

Since
(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n-1})

is a Gaussian random vector independent from
\bm{M}^{(l)}
but dependent
on
\lambda_{l}
, our proof strategy is to apply Lemma
20
.

To this end, recall the definition

\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)}:=(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}

and the eigen-decomposition of

\bm{M}^{(l)}=\bm{U}^{(l)}\bm{\Lambda}^{(l)}\bm{U}^{(l)\top}=\sum_{1\leq i<n}\lambda_{i}^{(l)}\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)\top}.

To begin with, we claim that with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
:

V:=\sup_{\lambda-\gamma(\lambda)\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}}}(\lambda_{l}^{\star})}\bigg\|\sum_{k:k\neq l}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\big(\lambda\bm{I}-\bm{M}^{(l)}\big)^{-1}\bigg\|_{2}\lesssim\sum_{k:k\neq l}\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}|\sqrt{r}}{|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{k}^{\star}|}+\frac{1}{|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|}.

(C.19)

whose proof is postponed to the end of the section. Consequently,
we can invoke standard Gaussian concentration inequalities to obtain:
with probability at least
1-O\big(\kappa^{-10}(\lambda_{\max}/\Delta_{l}^{\star})^{-20}n^{-20}\big)
,

\displaystyle\bigg|(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}\cdot\sum_{k:k\neq l}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star\top}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}\big(\lambda\bm{I}-\bm{M}^{(l)}\big)^{-1}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{\log\bigg(\frac{n\kappa\lambda_{\max}}{\Delta_{l}^{\star}}\bigg)}\cdot V.

In addition, we collect a basic fact regarding the derivatives of
matrices: for any invertible matrix
\bm{A}
,

\frac{\mathrm{d}\bm{A}^{-1}}{\mathrm{d}x}=-\bm{A}^{-1}\frac{\mathrm{d}\bm{A}}{\mathrm{d}x}\bm{A}^{-1}.

With this identity in mind, one can derive: with probability at least

1-O(n^{-10})
, for all
\lambda
with
\lambda-\gamma(\lambda)\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}}}(\lambda_{l}^{\star})
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\lambda}\sum_{k:k\neq l}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star\top}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}\big(\lambda\bm{I}-\bm{M}^{(l)}\big)^{-1}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigg|\sum_{k:k\neq l}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star\top}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}\big(\lambda\bm{I}-\bm{M}^{(l)}\big)^{-2}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\overset{}{\leq}n\cdot\max_{1\leq i<n}\frac{1}{(\lambda-\lambda_{i}^{(l)})^{2}}\cdot\bigg\|\sum_{k:k\neq l}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star\top}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}\bigg\|_{2}\cdot\big\|(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{\lesssim}n\cdot\frac{1}{\Delta_{l}^{\star 2}\wedge\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}\cdot\sum_{k:k\neq l}|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}|\,\big\|\bm{u}_{k}^{\star\top}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}\big\|_{2}\cdot\sigma\sqrt{n\log n}

\displaystyle\qquad\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\leq}n^{3/2}\cdot\frac{\max_{i:i\neq l}|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{i}^{\star}|}{\Delta_{l}^{\star 2}\wedge\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}\cdot\sigma\sqrt{\log n}\sum_{k:k\neq l}\frac{\big|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\big|}{|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{k}^{\star}|}

\displaystyle\qquad\overset{(\mathrm{iii})}{\lesssim}n^{3/2}\cdot\bigg(\frac{\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}}{\Delta_{l}^{\star 2}}+\kappa^{2}\bigg)\cdot\frac{1}{|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|}\cdot\sigma\sqrt{\log n}\sum_{k:k\neq l}\frac{\big|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\big|}{|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{k}^{\star}|}

\displaystyle\qquad\overset{(\mathrm{iv})}{\lesssim}n^{3/2}\cdot\frac{\kappa^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}}{\Delta_{l}^{\star 2}}\cdot\frac{1}{|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|}\cdot V.

Here, (i) arises from (
5.33
) in Lemma
8

and the standard Gaussian concentration inequality; (ii) holds since

\big\|\bm{u}_{k}^{\star\top}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}\big\|_{2}\leq\|\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\|_{2}\|\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}\|\leq 1
;
(iii) is due to
\max_{i:i\neq l}|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{i}^{\star}|\lesssim\lambda_{\max}^{\star}
;
(iv) uses the definition of
V
in (
C.19
).
Moreover, it is easy to see that
\{\lambda\colon\lambda-\gamma(\lambda)\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}}}(\lambda_{l}^{\star})\}\subset\big[\lambda_{l}^{\star}-|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|/3,\,\lambda_{l}^{\star}+|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|/3\big]
.
As a consequence, we invoke Lemma
20
and the union
bound to find: with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
:

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{k:k\neq l}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\big(\lambda_{l}\bm{I}-\bm{M}^{(l)}\big)^{-1}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}\Big|\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{r\log\bigg(\frac{n\kappa\lambda_{\max}}{\Delta_{l}^{\star}}\bigg)}\cdot V

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{r\log\bigg(\frac{n\kappa\lambda_{\max}}{\Delta_{l}^{\star}}\bigg)}\sum_{k:k\neq l}\frac{\big|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\big|}{|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{k}^{\star}|}+\frac{\sigma}{|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|}\sqrt{\log\bigg(\frac{n\kappa\lambda_{\max}}{\Delta_{l}^{\star}}\bigg)}

as claimed.

The remainder of this section amounts to establishing (
C.19
),
and we shall work under the event where Lemma
8

holds, which happens with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
. Note
that for any
\lambda
such that
\lambda-\gamma(\lambda)\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}}}(\lambda_{l}^{\star})
,
one can express

\displaystyle\bigg\|\sum_{k:k\neq l}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\big(\lambda\bm{I}-\bm{M}^{(l)}\big)^{-1}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\sum_{k:k\neq l}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{U}^{(l)}\big(\lambda\bm{I}-\bm{\Lambda}^{(l)}\big)^{-1}\bm{U}^{(l)\top}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\sum_{k:k\neq l}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{U}^{(l)}\big(\lambda\bm{I}-\bm{\Lambda}^{(l)}\big)^{-1}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\sum_{1\leq i<n}\bigg(\frac{1}{\lambda-\lambda_{i}^{(l)}}\sum_{k:k\neq l}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}\bigg)^{2}}.

(C.20)

In what follows, we shall control the sum over
i<r
and the sum
over
i\geq r
separately.

•

Let us consider the sum over
i\geq r
first. According to Lemma

3
, we know that
|\lambda-\lambda_{i}^{(l)}|\gtrsim|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|

for all
i\geq r
. This in turn yields

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sum_{r\leq i\leq n-1}\bigg(\frac{1}{\lambda-\lambda_{i}^{(l)}}\sum_{k:k\neq l}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}\bigg)^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|}\sqrt{\sum_{r\leq i\leq n-1}\bigg(\sum_{k:k\neq l}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}\bigg)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|}\Big\|\sum_{k:k\neq l}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{U}^{(l)}\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|}\|\bm{a}\|_{2}\cdot\Big\|\sum_{k:k\neq l}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\Big\|\cdot\|\bm{U}^{(l)}\|=\frac{1}{|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|}.

(C.21)

Here, we make use of the fact that
\|\bm{a}\|_{2}=1
,
\|\bm{U}^{(l)}\|=1

as well as
\big\|\sum_{k:k\neq l}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\big\|=1
,
since both
\{\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\}
and
\{\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)}\}_{k:k\neq l}

form orthonormal bases.

•

We then move on to the sum over the range
1\leq i<r
. Given that

\{\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}\}_{i}
are orthonormal, it is straightforward
to demonstrate that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sum_{1\leq i<r}\bigg(\frac{1}{\lambda-\lambda_{i}^{(l)}}\sum_{k:k\neq l}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}\bigg)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\sum_{1\leq i<r}\bigg(\frac{1}{\lambda-\lambda_{i}^{(l)}}\sum_{k:k\neq l}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}\bigg)\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\sum_{k:k\neq l}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\sum_{1\leq i<r}\frac{\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}}{\lambda-\lambda_{i}^{(l)}}\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k:k\neq l}\big|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\big|\cdot\bigg\|\sum_{1\leq i<r}\frac{\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}}{\lambda-\lambda_{i}^{(l)}}\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k:k\neq l}\big|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\big|\sqrt{\sum_{1\leq i<r}\bigg(\frac{\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}}{\lambda-\lambda_{i}^{(l)}}\bigg)^{2}}.

(C.22)

The preceding inequality motivates us to control the quantity
\sum_{1\leq i<r}\Big(\frac{\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}}{\lambda-\lambda_{i}^{(l)}}\Big)^{2}
.
Towards this, let us decompose it as follows

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i<r}\bigg(\frac{\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}}{\lambda-\lambda_{i}^{(l)}}\bigg)^{2}=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}_{1}}\bigg(\frac{\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}}{\lambda-\lambda_{i}^{(l)}}\bigg)^{2}+\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}_{2}}\bigg(\frac{\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}}{\lambda-\lambda_{i}^{(l)}}\bigg)^{2}.

Here, the sets
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}
are defined
respectively by

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\{1\leq i<r\mid\lambda_{i}^{(l)}-\gamma(\lambda_{i}^{(l)})\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{E}_{k}}(\lambda_{k}^{\star})\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\{1\leq i<r\mid\lambda_{i}^{(l)}-\gamma(\lambda_{i}^{(l)})\notin\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{E}_{k}}(\lambda_{k}^{\star})\},

where
\mathcal{E}_{k}:=c\,|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{k}^{\star}|

for some sufficiently small constant
c>0
. In the sequel, we shall
control these two sums separately.

–

For each
i\in\mathcal{A}_{1}
, we claim that

|\lambda-\lambda_{i}^{(l)}|\geq\big|\lambda-f^{-1}(\lambda_{k}^{\star})\big|-\big|f^{-1}(\lambda_{k}^{\star})-\lambda_{i}^{(l)}\big|\geq\frac{1}{2}\,\big|\lambda-f^{-1}(\lambda_{k}^{\star})\big|\gtrsim|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{k}^{\star}|.

(C.23)

To see this, arguing similarly as in the proof of Lemma
3
,
we can use the Lipschitz property of
f
(cf. (
C.4
))
to obtain

\displaystyle\big|\lambda-f^{-1}(\lambda_{k}^{\star})\big|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\,\big|f(\lambda)-f\big(f^{-1}(\lambda_{k}^{\star})\big)\big|=\frac{1}{2}\,\big|(\lambda-\gamma(\lambda))-\lambda_{k}^{\star}\big|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\,|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{k}^{\star}|-\frac{1}{2}\,\big|\lambda-\gamma(\lambda)-\lambda_{l}^{\star}\big|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\,|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{k}^{\star}|-\frac{1}{2}\,\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{k}^{\star}|.

In a similar manner, we can also derive

\big|f^{-1}(\lambda_{k}^{\star})-\lambda_{i}^{(l)}\big|\leq 2\,\big|f\big(f^{-1}(\lambda_{k}^{\star})\big)-f(\lambda_{i}^{(l)})\big|=2\,\big|\lambda_{k}^{\star}-\big(\lambda_{i}^{(l)}-\gamma(\lambda_{i}^{(l)})\big)\big|.

Therefore, for any
\lambda_{i}^{(l)}
such that
\lambda_{i}^{(l)}-\gamma(\lambda_{i}^{(l)})\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{E}_{k}}(\lambda_{k}^{\star})
,
one has

\big|f^{-1}(\lambda_{k}^{\star})-\lambda_{i}^{(l)}\big|\leq 2\,\mathcal{E}_{k}+2\,\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}}\leq 2c\,|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{k}^{\star}|\leq\frac{1}{2}\,\big|\lambda_{l}-f^{-1}(\lambda_{k}^{\star})\big|.

Then the claim is an immediate consequence of these two bounds. As
a result, we conclude that

\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}_{1}}\bigg(\frac{\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}}{\lambda-\lambda_{i}^{(l)}}\bigg)^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{k}^{\star})^{2}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}_{1}}\big(\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}\big)^{2}\leq\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{k}^{\star})^{2}}.

–

Turning to the set
\mathcal{A}_{2}
, we know from Lemma
3

that for any
i\in\mathcal{A}_{2}
,
|\lambda-\lambda_{i}^{(l)}|\gtrsim\min_{i:i\neq l}|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{i}^{\star}|

and

\Big\|\big(\lambda_{i}^{(l)}\bm{I}_{r-1}-\bm{\Lambda}^{\star(l)}-\gamma(\lambda_{i}^{(l)})\bm{I}_{r-1}\big)\bm{U}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i,\parallel}^{(l)}\Big\|_{2}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}}=\sigma\sqrt{r}\log n.

Meanwhile, since
\bm{u}_{i,\parallel}^{(l)}
is the projection of

\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}
onto the space of
\bm{U}^{\star(l)}
followed
by normalization, one has

\big|\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}\big|\leq\big|\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i,\parallel}^{(l)}\big|,

and therefore,

\displaystyle\Big\|\big(\lambda_{i}^{(l)}\bm{I}_{r-1}-\bm{\Lambda}^{\star(l)}-\gamma(\lambda_{i}^{(l)})\bm{I}_{r-1}\big)\bm{U}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i,\parallel}^{(l)}\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\big|\lambda_{i}^{(l)}-\lambda_{k}^{\star}-\gamma(\lambda_{i}^{(l)})\big|\cdot\big|\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i,\parallel}^{(l)}\big|

\displaystyle\geq\mathcal{E}_{k}\cdot\big|\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i,\parallel}^{(l)}\big|

\displaystyle\gtrsim\big|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{k}^{\star}\big|\cdot\big|\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i,\parallel}^{(l)}\big|.

This in turn allows us to derive that

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}}{\lambda-\lambda_{i}^{(l)}}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\frac{\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i,\parallel}^{(l)}}{\lambda-\lambda_{i}^{(l)}}\bigg)^{2}\leq\bigg(\frac{\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i,\parallel}^{(l)}}{\big(\Delta_{l}^{\star}\big)^{2}}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}r\log^{2}n}{|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{k}^{\star}|^{2}\big(\Delta_{l}^{\star}\big)^{2}}.

Putting the above two sums together reveals that

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i<r}\bigg(\frac{\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}}{\lambda-\lambda_{i}^{(l)}}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{k}^{\star})^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}r^{2}\log^{2}n}{|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{k}^{\star}|^{2}\big(\Delta_{l}^{\star}\big)^{2}}\lesssim\frac{r}{(\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{k}^{\star})^{2}},

where the last inequality follows since
\big(\Delta_{l}^{\star}\big)^{2}\gtrsim\sigma^{2}r\log^{2}n
.
Combining this inequality with (
C.22
),
one readily obtains

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sum_{1\leq i<r}\bigg(\frac{1}{\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{i}^{(l)}}\sum_{k:k\neq l}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}\bigg)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k:k\neq l}\big|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\big|\sqrt{\sum_{1\leq i<r}\bigg(\frac{\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}}{\lambda-\lambda_{i}^{(l)}}\bigg)^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k:k\neq l}\frac{\big|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\big|\sqrt{r}}{\left|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{k}^{\star}\right|}.

(C.24)

Substituting the above two partial sums (
C.21
)
and (
C.24
) into (
C.20
),
we conclude that: with probability exceeding
1-O(n^{-10})
,

\bigg\|\sum_{k:k\neq l}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star(l)\top}\big(\lambda_{l}\bm{I}_{n-1}-\bm{M}^{(l)}\big)^{-1}\bigg\|_{2}\lesssim\sum_{k:k\neq l}\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}^{\star}|\sqrt{r}}{|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{k}^{\star}|}+\frac{1}{|\lambda_{l}^{\star}|}

holds for any
\lambda
such that
\lambda-\gamma(\lambda)\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{MD}}}(\lambda_{l}^{\star})
,
as claimed in (
C.19
).

C.4
Proof of Lemma
6

Recall the definitions of
\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}
,
\bm{u}_{l,\perp}

and
\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}
in (
5.13
), (
5.24
)
and (
5.32
), respectively. Let us rewrite

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\bm{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star\perp}}\bm{a},\,\bm{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star\perp}}\bm{u}_{l}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{=}\big|\big\langle(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{a},\,(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{u}_{l}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle=\big|\big\langle(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{a},\,(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\top}\bm{u}_{l}+\bm{P}_{\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}}\bm{u}_{l})\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{=}\big|\big\langle(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{a},\,(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{P}_{\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}}\bm{u}_{l})\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{iii})}{=}\big|\big\langle(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{a},\,(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{u}_{l,\perp}\big\rangle\big|\cdot\big\|\bm{P}_{\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}}\bm{u}_{l}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{iv})}{=}\big|\big\langle(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp})^{\top}\bm{a},\,(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp})^{\top}\bm{u}_{l,\perp}\big\rangle\big|\cdot\big\|\bm{P}_{\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}}\bm{u}_{l}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\big|\big\langle(\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp})^{\top}\big((\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{a}\big),\,(\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp})^{\top}\big((\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{u}_{l,\perp}\big)\big\rangle\big|\cdot\big\|\bm{P}_{\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}}\bm{u}_{l}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{v})}{=}\big|\big\langle\bm{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}}\big((\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{a}\big),\,\bm{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}}\big((\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{u}_{l,\perp}\big)\big\rangle\big|\cdot\big\|\bm{P}_{\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}}\bm{u}_{l}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{vi})}{=}\frac{1}{\big\|\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{l,\perp}\big\|_{2}}\big|\big\langle\bm{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}}\big((\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{a}\big),\,\bm{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}}\big((\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{l,\perp}\big)\big\rangle\big|\cdot\big\|\bm{P}_{\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}}\bm{u}_{l}\big\|_{2}.

(C.25)

Here, (i) follows since
(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{U}^{\star\perp}=\bm{I}_{n-r}
;
(ii) holds true since
(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}=0
;
(iii) holds due to the definition
\bm{u}_{l,\perp}\coloneqq(\bm{P}_{\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}}\bm{u}_{l})/\|\bm{P}_{\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}}\bm{u}_{l}\|_{2}
;
(iv) results from the fact
\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}=\bm{U}^{\star\perp}
;
(v) holds true since
(\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp})^{\top}\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}=\bm{I}_{n-r}
;
(vi) arises from (
5.4c
) in Theorem
5
,
where we denote (i.e.,
\bm{u}_{l,\perp}
is the normalized version
of
\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{l,\perp}
)

\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{l,\perp}:=\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}\big(\lambda_{l}\bm{I}_{n-1}-(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{M}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}\big)^{-1}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{M}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}.

(C.26)

Our proof strategy is to show that
\frac{\bm{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}}\big((\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{l,\perp}\big)}{\big\|\bm{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}}\big((\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{l,\perp}\big)\big\|_{2}}

is a random vector uniformly distributed in the unit sphere of the
subspace
\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}
. If this claim were true, then
it would follow from standard measure concentration for uniform distributions
results
(
Vershynin 2017
, Theorem 3.4.6)
that, with probability
at least
1-O(n^{-10})
,

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\bm{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}}\big((\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{a}\big),\,\bm{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}}\big((\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{l,\perp}\big)\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n-r}}\,\big\|\bm{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}}\big((\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{a}\big)\big\|_{2}\big\|\bm{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}}\big((\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{l,\perp}\big)\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\,\big\|\bm{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star\perp}}\bm{a}\big\|_{2}\big\|\bm{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star\perp}}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{l,\perp}\big\|_{2},

where we use
\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}=\bm{U}^{\star\perp}

and the rank assumption
r\ll n/\log^{2}n
in the last step. Combining
this with (
C.25
), we arrive at the
advertised bound:

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\bm{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star\perp}}\bm{a},\,\bm{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star\perp}}\bm{u}_{l}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\,\frac{\big\|\bm{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star\perp}}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{l,\perp}\big\|_{2}}{\big\|\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{l,\perp}\big\|_{2}}\big\|\bm{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star\perp}}\bm{a}\big\|_{2}\big\|\bm{P}_{\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}}\bm{u}_{l}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\,\big\|\bm{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star\perp}}\bm{a}\big\|_{2}\big\|\bm{P}_{\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}}\bm{u}_{l}\big\|_{2}.

To justify the distributional property claimed above, we define — for
an arbitrary rotation matrix
\bm{Q}\in\mathbb{R}^{(n-r)\times(n-r)}

— a new rotation matrix

\bm{R}=\bm{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star(l)}}+\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}\bm{Q}\,(\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{(n-1)\times(n-1)};

the matrix
\bm{R}
rotates vectors in the subspace spanned by
\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}

according to
\bm{Q}
, while preserving the part in the subspace
spanned by
\bm{U}^{\star(l)}
. We make note of two important “rotational
invariance” properties as follows.

•

As shown in the proof of Lemma
3
,
it is seen that

\displaystyle\bm{R}\,(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}

\displaystyle=\bm{U}^{\star(l)}(\bm{U}^{\star(l)})^{\top}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}+\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}\bm{Q}\,(\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp})^{\top}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}

\displaystyle=(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{U}_{\smallsetminus l}^{\star}\bm{U}_{\smallsetminus l}^{\star\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}+(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{U}^{\star\perp}\bm{Q}\,(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{d}}{=}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{U}_{\smallsetminus l}^{\star}\bm{U}_{\smallsetminus l}^{\star\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}+(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{U}^{\star\perp}(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}

\displaystyle=(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\big(\bm{I}_{n}-\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\top}\big)\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}

\displaystyle=(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}.

Here, the second line arises from the definitions of
\bm{U}^{\star(l)}

and
\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}
in (
5.32
);
the third line follows because
\bm{Q}\,(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}\overset{\mathrm{d}}{=}(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}
;
the last line holds due to the fact
(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}=\bm{0}
.

•

In a similar manner, we also know that

\displaystyle\bm{R}\,(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}\bm{R}^{\top}

\displaystyle=(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\big(\bm{U}_{\smallsetminus l}^{\star}\bm{U}_{\smallsetminus l}^{\star\top}+\bm{U}^{\star\perp}\bm{Q}\,(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\big)\bm{H}\big(\bm{U}_{\smallsetminus l}^{\star}\bm{U}_{\smallsetminus l}^{\star\top}+\bm{U}^{\star\perp}\bm{Q}^{\top}\,(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\big)\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}

\displaystyle=(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\big(\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}+\bm{U}^{\star\perp}\bm{Q}\,(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\big)\bm{H}\big(\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}+\bm{U}^{\star\perp}\bm{Q}^{\top}\,(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\big)\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{d}}{=}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp},

where the second line comes from
(\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}-\bm{U}_{\smallsetminus l}^{\star}\bm{U}_{\smallsetminus l}^{\star\top})\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}=\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\top}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}=0
,
and the last line holds since
\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}+\bm{U}^{\star\perp}\bm{Q}^{\top}\,(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}

is a rotation matrix.

Using the statistical independence between these two parts, we reach

\displaystyle\bm{R}\,(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{l,\perp}

\displaystyle=\bm{R}\,\big(\lambda_{l}\bm{I}_{n-1}-(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{M}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}\big)^{-1}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{M}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}=\big(\lambda_{l}\bm{I}_{n-1}-\bm{R}\,(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{M}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}\bm{R}^{\top}\big)^{-1}\bm{R}\,(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{M}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}

\displaystyle=\big(\lambda_{l}\bm{I}_{n-1}-\bm{R}\,(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}\bm{R}^{\top}\big)^{-1}\bm{R}\,(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{d}}{=}\big(\lambda_{l}\bm{I}_{n-1}-(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}\big)^{-1}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}

\displaystyle=\big(\lambda_{l}\bm{I}_{n-1}-(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{M}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}\big)^{-1}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{M}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star}

\displaystyle=(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{l,\perp},

where the last step replies on the definition of
\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{l,\perp}

in (
C.26
) and the fact
(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}=\bm{I}_{n-1}
.
This enables us to conclude that
\frac{\bm{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}}\big((\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{l,\perp}\big)}{\big\|\bm{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}}\big((\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{l,\perp}\big)\big\|_{2}}

is uniformly distributed in the unit sphere spanned by
\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}
.

Appendix D
Proof of auxiliary lemmas in the analysis for Theorem
3

D.1
Proof of Lemma
7

For any matrix
\bm{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
, we know from the
orthogonal invariance of the spectral norm that

\displaystyle\|\bm{A}\|

\displaystyle=\|[\bm{U}^{\star},\bm{U}^{\star\perp}]^{\top}\bm{A}[\bm{U}^{\star},\bm{U}^{\star\perp}]\|

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\begin{bmatrix}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\bm{A}\bm{U}^{\star}&\bm{U}^{\star\top}\bm{A}\bm{U}^{\star\perp}\\
(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{U}^{\star}&(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{U}^{\star\perp}\end{bmatrix}\bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{U}^{\star\top}\bm{A}\bm{U}^{\star}\|+\|\bm{U}^{\star\top}\bm{A}\bm{U}^{\star\perp}\|+\|(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{U}^{\star}\|+\|(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{U}^{\star\perp}\|,

where the last step holds due to the triangle inequality. As a result,
one can upper bound

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}\bm{S}^{\top}-\bm{\Sigma}\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|\bm{U}^{\star\top}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}\bm{S}^{\top}-\bm{\Sigma}\Big)\bm{U}^{\star}\Big\|+\Big\|(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}\bm{S}^{\top}-\bm{\Sigma}\Big)\bm{U}^{\star}\Big\|

\displaystyle\quad+\Big\|\bm{U}^{\star\top}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}\bm{S}^{\top}-\bm{\Sigma}\Big)\bm{U}^{\star\perp}\Big\|+\Big\|(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}\bm{S}^{\top}-\bm{\Sigma}\Big)\bm{U}^{\star\perp}\Big\|

\displaystyle=\Big\|\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{\parallel}\bm{S}_{\parallel}^{\top}-\bm{\Lambda}\Big\|+2\,\Big\|\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{\perp}\bm{S}_{\parallel}^{\top}\Big\|+\Big\|\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{\perp}\bm{S}_{\perp}^{\top}-\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{p-r}\Big\|

(D.1)

where we remind the readers of the notation
\bm{S}_{\parallel}:=\bm{U}^{\star\top}\bm{S}
,

\bm{S}_{\perp}:=(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{S}
and
\bm{\Lambda}:=\bm{U}^{\star\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{U}^{\star}

introduced in (
5.46
).

Before describing how to control these quantities, we pause to collect
a few results regarding a Gaussian random matrix
\bm{G}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times n}

consisting of i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
entries
(
Vershynin 2017
, Theorem 4.6.1)
:
with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
,

\displaystyle\|\bm{G}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{p}+\sqrt{n},

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{1}{n}\bm{G}\bm{G}^{\top}-\bm{I}_{p}\Big\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}+\frac{p}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}},

(D.2)

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{1}{n}\bm{G}^{\top}\bm{G}-\frac{p}{n}\bm{I}_{n}\Big\|

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}.

(D.3)

With these bounds in place, we can start to bound the spectral norms
of the quantities in (
D.1
). Note that
the columns of
\bm{S}_{\parallel}
(resp.
\bm{S}_{\perp}
) are
i.i.d. zero-mean Gaussian random vectors with covariance
\bm{\Lambda}

(resp.
\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{p-r}
). Since we can rewrite
\bm{S}_{\parallel}=\bm{\Lambda}^{1/2}\bm{Z}

with
\bm{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times n}
being a Gaussian random matrix
with i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
entries, it immediately follows from
(
D.2
) that with probability more than
1-O(n^{-10})
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{\perp}\bm{S}_{\perp}^{\top}-\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{p-r}\Big\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{2}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}+\frac{p}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\bigg)\qquad\text{and}

(D.4)

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{\parallel}\bm{S}_{\parallel}^{\top}-\bm{\Lambda}\Big\|

\displaystyle=\Big\|\frac{1}{n}\bm{\Lambda}^{1/2}\bm{Z}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{1/2}-\bm{\Lambda}\Big\|\leq\|\bm{\Lambda}\|\Big\|\frac{1}{n}\bm{Z}\bm{Z}^{\top}-\bm{I}_{r}\Big\|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\lambda_{\max}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{r}{n}}+\frac{r}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\lambda_{\max}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\sqrt{\frac{r\log n}{n}},

(D.5)

where the last step arises from the sample size assumption
n\geq r
.
As for
\bm{S}_{\perp}\bm{S}_{\parallel}^{\top}
, we can invoke Lemma
19

to show that: with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{\perp}\bm{S}_{\parallel}^{\top}\Big\|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\big\|\bm{S}_{\perp}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{1/2}\big\|\leq\frac{1}{n}\big\|\bm{S}_{\perp}\bm{Z}^{\top}\big\|\,\big\|\bm{\Lambda}^{1/2}\big\|\lesssim\frac{\sigma}{n}\big(\sqrt{pn\log n}+\sqrt{pr}\log n\big)\cdot\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}^{\star}+\sigma^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{(\lambda_{\max}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\sigma^{2}\frac{p}{n}}\log n,

(D.6)

where the last step holds since
n\geq r
. Putting the bounds above
together immediately concludes the proof.

D.2
Proof of Lemma
8

The proof of this lemma is similar to that of Lemma
3
.
By definition, one can compute

\displaystyle\bm{\Sigma}_{l,\perp}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{S}_{l,\perp}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}^{\top}\big]

(D.7)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{S}\bm{S}^{\top}\big]\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}=(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}

\displaystyle=(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{\Lambda}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}+\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{p-1}

\displaystyle=\bm{U}^{\star(l)}\bm{\Lambda}^{\star(l)}\bm{U}^{\star(l)\top}+\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{p-1},

where
\bm{U}^{\star(l)}
and
\bm{\Lambda}^{\star(l)}
have been
defined in (
5.72
). Our proof strategy
is to invoke Theorem
6
by treating

\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}^{\top}
(resp.
\bm{U}^{\star(l)}
)
as
\bm{M}
(resp.
\bm{Q}
).

•

We shall start with the first claim. Let us define the following matrices
in
\mathbb{R}^{(r-1)\times(r-1)}
:

\displaystyle\bm{K}^{(l)}(\lambda)

\displaystyle:=\bm{U}^{\star(l)\top}\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}^{\top}\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}\Big(\lambda\bm{I}_{p-r}-(\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp})^{\top}\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}^{\top}\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}\Big)^{-1}(\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp})^{\top}\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}^{\top}\bm{U}^{\star(l)},

\displaystyle\bm{K}^{(l)\perp}(\lambda)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\Big[\bm{G}^{(l)}(\lambda)\mid(\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp})^{\top}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}^{\top}\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}\Big].

Recall the definitions of
\bm{K}(\lambda)
(cf. (
5.53
))
and
\bm{P}^{(l)}
(cf. (
C.6
)), and notice that

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp}\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}

\displaystyle=\bm{U}^{\star\perp},

\displaystyle(\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp})^{\top}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}

\displaystyle=(\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp})^{\top}(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{S}=(\bm{U}^{\star\perp})^{\top}\bm{S}=\bm{S}_{\perp}.

Straightforward calculation allows us to simplify the above expressions
as follows

\displaystyle\bm{K}^{(l)}(\lambda)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\bm{P}^{(l)\top}\bm{S}_{\parallel}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{\perp}^{\top}\Big(\lambda\bm{I}_{p-r}-\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{\perp}\bm{S}_{\perp}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}\bm{S}_{\perp}\cdot\bm{S}_{\parallel}^{\top}\bm{P}^{(l)}=\bm{P}^{(l)\top}\bm{K}(\lambda)\bm{P}^{(l)},

\displaystyle\bm{K}^{(l)\perp}(\lambda)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{G}^{(l)}(\lambda)\mid(\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp})^{\top}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}^{\top}\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp}\big]=\beta(\lambda)\bm{P}^{(l)\top}(\bm{\Lambda}^{\star}+\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{r})\bm{P}^{(l)}=\beta(\lambda)(\bm{\Lambda}^{\star(l)}+\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{r-1}).

Theorem
6
then tells us that

\displaystyle\big(\gamma_{i}^{(l)}\bm{I}_{r-1}-\bm{\Lambda}^{\star(l)}-\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{r-1}-\bm{K}^{(l)\perp}(\gamma_{i}^{(l)})\big)\bm{U}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i,\parallel}^{(l)}

\displaystyle\qquad=\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{P}^{(l)\top}\bm{S}_{\parallel}\bm{S}_{\parallel}^{\top}\bm{P}^{(l)}-\bm{\Lambda}^{\star(l)}-\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{r-1}+\bm{K}(\gamma_{i}^{(l)})-\bm{K}^{\perp}(\gamma_{i}^{(l)})\Big)\bm{U}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i,\parallel}^{(l)}.

One can then adopt a similar argument as in the proof of Theorem
8

to demonstrate that: with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\Big(\gamma_{i}^{(l)}\bm{I}_{r-1}-\big(1+\beta(\gamma_{i}^{(l)})\big)\big(\bm{\Lambda}^{\star(l)}+\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{r-1}\big)\Big)\bm{U}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i,\parallel}^{(l)}\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\Big\|\frac{1}{n}\bm{P}^{(l)\top}\bm{S}_{\parallel}\bm{S}_{\parallel}^{\top}\bm{P}^{(l)}-\bm{\Lambda}^{\star(l)}-\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{r-1}\Big\|+\sup_{\lambda:\lambda\in[2\lambda_{l}^{\star}/3,\,4\lambda_{l}^{\star}/3]}\Big\|\big(\bm{K}(\gamma_{i}^{(l)})-\bm{K}^{\perp}(\gamma_{i}^{(l)})\big)\bm{U}^{\star(l)\top}\bm{u}_{i,\parallel}^{(l)}\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\Big\|\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{\parallel}\bm{S}_{\parallel}^{\top}-\bm{\Lambda}^{\star}-\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{r}\Big\|+\sup_{\lambda:\lambda\in[2\lambda_{l}^{\star}/3,\,4\lambda_{l}^{\star}/3]}\Big\|\bm{K}(\gamma_{i}^{(l)})-\bm{K}^{\perp}(\gamma_{i}^{(l)})\Big\|\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{PCA}}

and there exists some
k\neq l
(
1\leq k\leq r
) obeying

\bigg|\frac{\gamma_{i}^{(l)}}{1+\beta(\gamma_{i}^{(l)})}-\lambda_{k}^{\star}-\sigma^{2}\bigg|\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{PCA}}.

•

Next, we turn to the second claim and we shall prove the upper and
lower bounds for
\gamma_{i}^{(l)}
separately.

–

For the upper bound, it suffices to upper bound
\gamma_{r}^{(l)}

since
\{\gamma_{i}^{(l)}\}_{i}
are defined in descending order.
In view of
(\bm{U}^{\star(l)\perp})^{\top}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}=\bm{S}_{\perp}
,
we can invoke Lemma
21
to see that

\gamma_{r}^{(l)}\leq\lambda_{1}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{\perp}\bm{S}_{\perp}^{\top}\Big).

This suggests that we look at the spectrum of
\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{\perp}\bm{S}_{\perp}^{\top}
.
From (
D.2
) and (
D.3
), we can obtain

\Big|\lambda_{i}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{\perp}\bm{S}_{\perp}^{\top}\Big)-\sigma^{2}\frac{n\vee(p-r)}{n}\Big|\lesssim\sigma^{2}\sqrt{\frac{p+\log n}{n}},\qquad 1\leq i\leq(p-r)\wedge n,

where we use the fact that the non-zero eigenvalues of
\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{\perp}\bm{S}_{\perp}^{\top}

and
\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{S}_{\perp}
are identical.
Therefore, one has

\gamma_{i}^{(l)}\leq\gamma_{r}^{(l)}\leq\sigma^{2}(n\vee p)/n+O(\sigma^{2}\sqrt{(p+\log n)/n})

for all
r\leq i\leq n\wedge(p-1)
.

–

Next, we move on to consider the lower bound. Observe that the matrix

\bm{\Sigma}_{l,\perp}
defined in (
D.7
)
satisfies the following properties: (i)
\bm{\Sigma}_{l,\perp}\succeq\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{p-1}
;
(ii)
\bm{\Sigma}_{l,\perp}^{-1/2}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}
is a Gaussian
random matrix composed of i.i.d. standard Gaussian entries. Then
we can lower bound the eigenvalues
\gamma_{i}^{(l)}
for any
1\leq i\leq(p-1)\wedge n

as follows

\displaystyle\gamma_{i}^{(l)}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{i}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}^{\top}\Big)\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{=}\lambda_{i}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}^{\top}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}\Big)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{i}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\big(\bm{\Sigma}_{l,\perp}^{-1/2}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}\big)^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{l,\perp}\bm{\Sigma}_{l,\perp}^{-1/2}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\geq}\sigma^{2}\lambda_{i}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\big(\bm{\Sigma}_{l,\perp}^{-1/2}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}\big)^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{l,\perp}^{-1/2}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{iii})}{=}\sigma^{2}\lambda_{i}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{\Sigma}_{l,\perp}^{-1/2}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}\big(\bm{\Sigma}_{l,\perp}^{-1/2}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}\big)^{\top}\Big),

where (i) and (iii) hold because
i\leq(p-1)\wedge n
; (ii) follows
since for any matrix
\bm{A}
, one has
\bm{A}^{\top}(\bm{\Sigma}_{l,\perp}-\sigma^{2}\bm{I})\bm{A}\succeq 0

and hence
\lambda_{i}(\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{l,\perp}\bm{A})\geq\sigma^{2}\lambda_{i}(\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{A})
.
By invoking (
D.2
) and (
D.3
) once
again, we arrive at

\Big|\lambda_{i}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{\Sigma}_{l,\perp}^{-1/2}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}\big(\bm{\Sigma}_{l,\perp}^{-1/2}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}\big)^{\top}\Big)-\sigma^{2}\frac{n\vee(p-1)}{n}\Big|\lesssim\sigma^{2}\sqrt{\frac{p+\log n}{n}},\qquad 1\leq i\leq n\wedge(p-1).

This immediately establishes the claimed lower bound.

•

The third claim is an immediate consequence of the fact that

\mathsf{rank}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}^{\top}\Big)\leq n\wedge(p-1).

•

Finally, let us consider the last claim. In view of Theorem
8
,
we have

\frac{\lambda_{l}}{1+\beta(\lambda_{l})}\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{PCA}}}(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2}).

In addition, the first claim asserts that for each
1\leq i<r
, one
has

\frac{\gamma_{i}^{(l)}}{1+\beta(\gamma_{i}^{(l)})}\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{PCA}}}(\lambda_{k}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})

for some
k\neq l
. In view of the Lipschitz property of the function

f(\lambda):=\frac{\lambda}{1+\beta(\lambda)}
(so that
|f^{\prime}(\lambda)|\lesssim 1
),
applying a similar argument as in the proof of Lemma
3

(see Appendix
C.1
)
immediately allows us to establish the claim.

D.3
Proof of Lemma
9

Before continuing, we introduce several useful notation that will
be used throughout.

•

Let
\bm{U}^{(l)}\sqrt{\bm{\Gamma}^{(l)}}\bm{V}^{(l)\top}
denote
the SVD of
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}
, where
\bm{\Gamma}^{(l)}

is a diagonal matrix consisting of the singular values of interest.
Here, we recall that
\bm{S}_{l,\perp}
has been defined in (
5.67
).

•

Let
\bm{u}_{i}^{(l)}
(resp.
\bm{v}_{i}^{(l)}
) indicate the

i
-th column of
\bm{U}^{(l)}
(resp.
\bm{V}^{(l)}
), and let

\gamma_{i}^{(l)}
represent the
i
-th diagonal entry of
\bm{\Gamma}^{(l)}
.

In addition, we note that the vector
\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}
(see
(
5.67
)) obeys

\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top}\sim\mathcal{N}\big(\bm{0},(\bm{u}_{l}^{\star\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{u}_{l}^{\star})\bm{I}_{n}\big)=\mathcal{N}\big(\bm{0},(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\bm{I}_{n}\big)

and is independent of
\bm{S}_{l,\perp}
(and thus
\bm{\Gamma}^{(l)}

and
\bm{V}^{(l)}
). This implies that condition on
\bm{V}^{(l)}
,
one has

\bm{v}_{i}^{(l)\top}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2}\right),\qquad 1\leq i<p.

(D.8)

Moreover, by virtue of the rotational invariance of i.i.d. Gaussian
random matrices, it is readily seen that
\bm{\Gamma}^{(l)}
is independent
of
\bm{V}^{(l)}
.

Now, we can begin to present the proof, towards which we start with
the following decomposition

\displaystyle\Big\|\Big(\lambda_{l}\bm{I}_{p-1}-\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top}\Big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{n}\Big\|\big(\lambda_{l}\bm{I}_{p-1}-\bm{U}^{(l)}\bm{\Gamma}^{(l)}\bm{U}^{(l)\top}\big)^{-1}\bm{U}^{(l)}\sqrt{\bm{\Gamma}^{(l)}}\bm{V}^{(l)\top}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top}\Big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{n}\Big\|\bm{U}^{(l)}\big(\lambda_{l}\bm{I}_{p-1}-\bm{\Gamma}^{(l)}\big)^{-1}\bm{U}^{(l)\top}\bm{U}^{(l)}\sqrt{\bm{\Gamma}^{(l)}}\bm{V}^{(l)\top}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top}\Big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{n}\Big\|\big(\lambda_{l}\bm{I}_{p-1}-\bm{\Gamma}^{(l)}\big)^{-1}\sqrt{\bm{\Gamma}^{(l)}}\bm{V}^{(l)\top}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top}\Big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{1\leq i\leq n\wedge(p-1)}\frac{\gamma_{i}^{(l)}}{(\lambda_{l}-\gamma_{i}^{(l)})^{2}}\big(\bm{v}_{i}^{(l)\top}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top}\big)^{2}.

(D.9)

In what follows, we shall control the sum over
i<r
and the sum
over
i\geq r
separately.

Controlling the sum over
i<r
.

According to Lemma
8
, one has

\gamma_{i}^{(l)}\lesssim\lambda_{\max}^{\star}+\sigma^{2}\qquad\text{and}\qquad(\gamma_{i}^{(l)}-\lambda_{l})^{2}\gtrsim\min_{i:i\neq l}(\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{i}^{\star})^{2}

for all
1\leq i<r
. In addition, recall that
\bm{v}_{i}^{(l)\top}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top}\,(1\leq i<p)

are i.i.d. zero-mean Gaussian random variables with variance
\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2}

(see (
D.8
)). Invoking standard Gaussian
inequalities shows that with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
,

\max_{1\leq i<r}\big(\bm{v}_{i}^{(l)\top}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top}\big)^{2}\lesssim(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\log n.

(D.10)

As a result, we obtain

\sum_{1\leq i<r}\frac{\gamma_{i}^{(l)}\big(\bm{v}_{i}^{(l)\top}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top}\big)^{2}}{(\lambda_{l}-\gamma_{i}^{(l)})^{2}}\lesssim\frac{(\lambda_{\max}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\,r\log n}{\big(\Delta_{l}^{\star}\big)^{2}}

(D.11)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
.

Controlling the sum over
i\geq r
.

Now, let us control the sum over
i\geq r
, and we shall consider
the case with
n\geq p
and the case with
n<p
separately.

•

Case I:
n\geq p
.
Note that
\bm{v}_{i}^{(l)\top}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})
.
This suggests that we decompose

\displaystyle\sum_{r\leq i\leq n\wedge(p-1)}\frac{\gamma_{i}^{(l)}(\bm{v}_{i}^{(l)\top}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top})^{2}}{(\lambda_{l}-\gamma_{i}^{(l)})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum_{r\leq i\leq n\wedge(p-1)}\frac{\gamma_{i}^{(l)}(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})}{(\lambda_{l}-\gamma_{i}^{(l)})^{2}}}_{=:\,\alpha_{1}}+\underbrace{\sum_{r\leq i\leq n\wedge(p-1)}\frac{\gamma_{i}^{(l)}\big((\bm{v}_{i}^{(l)\top}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top})^{2}-(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\big)}{(\lambda_{l}-\gamma_{i}^{(l)})^{2}}}_{=:\,\alpha_{2}},

(D.12)

and control
\alpha_{1}
as well as
\alpha_{2}
individually.

–

To begin with, let us consider
\alpha_{1}
, which requires estimating

\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2}
and
\sum_{r\leq i\leq n\wedge(p-1)}\gamma_{i}^{(l)}/(\lambda_{l}-\gamma_{i}^{(l)})^{2}
.
This task is accomplished in Lemma
15

and Lemma
16
stated below.

Lemma 15
.

Instate the assumptions
of Theorem
3
. With probability
at least
1-O(n^{-10})
, we have

\bigg|\underbrace{\sum_{i\geq r}\frac{\gamma_{i}^{(l)}}{(\lambda_{l}-\gamma_{i}^{(l)})^{2}}-\sum_{i>r}\frac{\lambda_{i}}{(\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{i})^{2}}}_{=:\,\epsilon_{1}}\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}\Big(1+\frac{p}{n}\Big).

(D.13)

and

\bigg|\sum_{i>r}\frac{\lambda_{i}}{(\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{i})^{2}}\bigg|\vee\bigg|\sum_{i\geq r}\frac{\gamma_{i}^{(l)}}{(\lambda_{l}-\gamma_{i}^{(l)})^{2}}\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}p}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}},

(D.14)

Proof.

See Appendix
D.3.1
.
∎

Lemma 16
.

Instate the assumptions
of Theorem
3
. With probability
at least
1-O(n^{-10})
, one has

\displaystyle\bigg|\underbrace{\frac{\lambda_{l}}{1+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i>r}\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{i}}}-(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})}_{=:\,\epsilon_{2}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\underbrace{(\lambda_{\max}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\sqrt{\frac{r\log n}{n}}}_{=\,\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{PCA}}}.

(D.15)

and

\frac{\lambda_{l}}{1+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i>r}\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{i}}}\asymp\lambda_{l}^{\star}.

(D.16)

Proof.

See Appendix
D.3.2
.
∎

With these two lemmas in place, we are ready to control
\alpha_{1}
.
According to (
D.14
), we have

\alpha_{1}\lesssim\frac{(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\sigma^{2}p}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}.

(D.17)

In addition, recall the definition of
c_{l}
(cf. (
15
)).
We can upper bound

\displaystyle|\alpha_{1}-c_{l}\cdot n|

\displaystyle=\bigg|(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\sum_{i\geq r}\frac{\gamma_{i}^{(l)}}{(\lambda_{l}-\gamma_{i}^{(l)})^{2}}-c_{l}\cdot n\bigg|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\bigg(\sum_{i>r}\frac{\lambda_{i}}{(\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{i})^{2}}+\epsilon_{1}\bigg)-(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2}+\epsilon_{2})\sum_{i>r}\frac{\lambda_{i}}{(\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{i})^{2}}\Bigg|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\epsilon_{1}(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})-\epsilon_{2}\sum_{i>r}\frac{\lambda_{i}}{(\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{i})^{2}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{\lesssim}(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})|\epsilon_{1}|+\frac{\sigma^{2}p}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}|\epsilon_{2}|

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\lesssim}(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\cdot\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}\Big(1+\frac{p}{n}\Big)+\frac{\sigma^{2}p}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}\cdot(\lambda_{\max}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\sqrt{\frac{r\log n}{n}}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}+\frac{(\lambda_{\max}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\sigma^{2}p}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}\sqrt{\frac{r\log n}{n}}.

(D.18)

Here, (i) makes use of (
D.14
) and (
D.13
);
(ii) relies on (
D.13
) and (
D.15
).

–

Next, we move on to look at
\alpha_{2}
. Observe that
\big\{(\bm{v}_{i}^{(l)\top}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top})^{2}-(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\big\}_{i\geq r}

is a sequence of zero-mean sub-exponential random variables, which
is independent of
\bm{\Gamma}^{(l)}
but depends on
\lambda_{l}
.
Hence, we shall apply the epsilon-net argument (cf. Lemma
20
)
to bound
\alpha_{2}
. To do so, let us first verify the conditions
required therein. With probability exceeding
1-O(n^{-20})
, one
has

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\lambda}\sum_{r\leq i\leq n\wedge(p-1)}\frac{\gamma_{i}^{(l)}}{(\lambda-\gamma_{i}^{(l)})^{2}}\big((\bm{v}_{i}^{(l)\top}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top})^{2}-(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\big)\Bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad=\Bigg|\sum_{r\leq i\leq n\wedge(p-1)}\frac{\gamma_{i}^{(l)}}{(\lambda-\gamma_{i}^{(l)})^{3}}\big((\bm{v}_{i}^{(l)\top}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top})^{2}-(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\big)\Bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq(p\wedge n)\cdot\max_{r\leq i\leq n\wedge(p-1)}\frac{\gamma_{i}^{(l)}}{(\lambda-\gamma_{i}^{(l)})^{3}}\cdot\max_{r\leq i\leq n\wedge(p-1)}\big|(\bm{v}_{i}^{(l)\top}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top})^{2}-(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\big|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim(p\wedge n)\cdot\frac{\sigma^{2}(p\vee n)}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 3}n}\cdot(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\log n

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\sigma^{2}p\log n}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 3}}

for all
\lambda
with
\lambda/\big(1+\beta(\lambda)\big)\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{PCA}}}(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})
.
In addition, it is seen that

\displaystyle\big\|(\bm{v}_{i}^{(l)\top}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top})^{2}-(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\big\|_{\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big((\bm{v}_{i}^{(l)\top}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top})^{2}-(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})^{2},

where
\|\cdot\|_{\psi_{1}}
denotes the sub-exponential norm. Invoke
the matrix Bernstein inequality
(
Koltchinskii 2011
, Corollary 2.1)

to show that: for any fixed
\lambda
obeying
\lambda/\big(1+\beta(\lambda)\big)\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{PCA}}}(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})
,
one has

\displaystyle\Bigg|\sum_{r\leq i\text{$\leq$}n\wedge(p-1)}\frac{\gamma_{i}^{(l)}}{(\lambda-\gamma_{i}^{(l)})^{2}}\big((\bm{v}_{i}^{(l)\top}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top})^{2}-(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\big)\Bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\max_{r\leq i\text{$\leq$}n\wedge(p-1)}\frac{\gamma_{i}^{(l)}}{(\lambda-\gamma_{i}^{(l)})^{2}}\cdot(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})(\log n+\sqrt{p\wedge n}\log n)

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\frac{(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}\frac{p\vee n}{n}\sqrt{p\wedge n}\log n

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\frac{(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\sigma^{2}p}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{p\wedge n}}

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
, where the last inequalities
hold since
|\lambda-\gamma_{i}^{(l)}|\gtrsim\lambda_{l}^{\star}

and
\gamma_{i}^{(l)}\lesssim\sigma^{2}(1+p/n)
. In addition, we
make the observation that

\{\lambda\colon\lambda/\big(1+\beta(\lambda)\big)\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{PCA}}}(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\}\subseteq[2\lambda_{l}^{\star}/3,\,4\lambda_{l}^{\star}/3].

With these in place, one can readily invoke Lemma
20

to derive

\alpha_{2}\lesssim\frac{(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\sigma^{2}p}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}}\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{p\wedge n}}

(D.19)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
.

–

Combining (
D.17
) and (
D.19
),
we conclude

\displaystyle\sum_{r\leq i\leq n\wedge(p-1)}\frac{\gamma_{i}^{(l)}(\bm{v}_{i}^{(l)\top}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top})^{2}}{n\,(\lambda_{l}-\gamma_{i}^{(l)})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|\alpha_{1}|+|\alpha_{2}|}{n}\lesssim\frac{(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\sigma^{2}p}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}n}+\frac{(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\sigma^{2}p}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}n}\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{p\wedge n}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\sigma^{2}p\log n}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}n}.

(D.20)

Moreover, putting (
D.18
) and (
D.19
)
together gives

\displaystyle\Bigg|\sum_{i\geq r}\frac{\gamma_{i}^{(l)}(\bm{v}_{i}^{(l)\top}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top})^{2}}{n\,(\lambda_{l}-\gamma_{i}^{(l)})^{2}}-c_{l}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|\alpha_{1}-c_{l}\cdot n|+|\alpha_{2}|}{n}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}n}+\frac{(\lambda_{\max}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\sigma^{2}p}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}n}\sqrt{\frac{r\log n}{n}}+\frac{(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\sigma^{2}p}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}n}\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{p\wedge n}}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{\sigma^{2}p}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}n}\bigg((\lambda_{\max}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\sqrt{\frac{r\log n}{n}}+(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{p\wedge n}}\bigg),

(D.21)

where
c_{l}
is defined in (
3.12
).

•

Case II:
n<p
.
As it turns out, the above analysis for (
D.21
)
is not tight when it comes to the case
n<p
. To remedy the issue,
we provide a more precise estimate for terms (
D.12
)
in the following lemma.

Lemma 17
.

Instate the assumptions
of Theorem
3
. Suppose that
p>n
,
then the following holds with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
:

\displaystyle\sum_{i\geq r}\frac{\gamma_{i}^{(l)}(\bm{v}_{i}^{(l)\top}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top})^{2}}{(\lambda_{l}-\gamma_{i}^{(l)})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{\sigma^{2}p}{\lambda_{l}-\sigma^{2}p/n}+\frac{\lambda_{l}}{\lambda_{l}-\sigma^{2}p/n}\ \frac{\lambda_{l}}{1+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i>r}\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{i}}}\sum_{r<i\leq n}\frac{\lambda_{i}-\sigma^{2}p/n}{(\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{i})^{2}}}_{=c_{l}\cdot n}

\displaystyle\quad+O\Big(\frac{\sigma^{2}pr\log n}{\min_{i:i\neq l}\left|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{i}^{\star}\right|n}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\kappa\sqrt{pr\log n}}{\lambda_{l}^{\star}}\Big).

(D.22)

Proof.

See Appendix
D.3.3
.
∎

Here, the quantity
c_{l}
introduced above is precisely the one
defined in (
3.12
). Consequently, we arrive at

\displaystyle\Bigg|\sum_{i\geq r}\frac{\gamma_{i}^{(l)}(\bm{v}_{i}^{(l)\top}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top})^{2}}{n\,(\lambda_{l}-\gamma_{i}^{(l)})^{2}}-c_{l}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}pr\log n}{\min_{i:i\neq l}\left|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{i}^{\star}\right|n}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\kappa\sqrt{pr\log n}}{\lambda_{l}^{\star}n}.

(D.23)

Combining two sums.

Substituting (
D.11
) and (
D.20
)
(which holds universally for any
n
) into (
D.9
),
we reach the first claim (
5.76
):

\Big\|\Big(\lambda_{l}\bm{I}_{p-1}-\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\bm{S}_{l,\perp}\bm{s}_{l,\parallel}^{\top}\Big\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\frac{(\lambda_{\max}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\,r\log n}{\min_{i:i\neq l}\left|\lambda_{l}^{\star}-\lambda_{i}^{\star}\right|^{2}n}+\frac{(\lambda_{l}^{\star}+\sigma^{2})\sigma^{2}p\log^{2}n}{\lambda_{l}^{\star 2}n}\ll 1,
