dividing into two cases based on the relative size of
\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\star}\cos\theta
compared to
|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{\perp}|\sin\theta
. Without loss of generality, let us assume that
\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\star}\geq 0
in the sequel, and recall that
\theta\in[0,\pi/2]
.

•

In the case where
\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\star}\cos\theta\geq|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{\perp}|\sin\theta
, one can demonstrate that

\displaystyle\max_{s^{\prime}=\pm 1}\Big|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\star}-\big|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\star}\cos\theta+s^{\prime}\,|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{\perp}|\sin\theta\big|\Big|

\displaystyle=\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\star}(1-\cos\theta)+|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{\perp}|\sin\theta

\displaystyle\geq(1-\cos\theta)|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\star}|\geq\frac{1-\cos^{2}\theta}{2}|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\star}|

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{\sigma^{2}n}{\lambda_{1}^{\star 2}}\left|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\star}\right|

with high probability, where the last step results from (
5.36
).

•

On the other hand, if instead
\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\star}\cos\theta<|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{\perp}|\sin\theta
, then one can deduce that

\displaystyle\max_{s^{\prime}=\pm 1}\Big|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\star}-\big|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\star}\cos\theta+s^{\prime}\,\big|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{\perp}|\sin\theta\big|\Big|

\displaystyle=\max_{s^{\prime}=\pm 1}\Big|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\star}-\big|s^{\prime}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\star}\cos\theta+|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{\perp}|\sin\theta\big|\Big|

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{=}\max_{s^{\prime}=\pm 1}\Big|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\star}-|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{\perp}|\sin\theta-s^{\prime}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\star}\cos\theta\Big|

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{\geq}\left|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\star}\right|\cos\theta\overset{\mathrm{(iii)}}{\asymp}\left|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\star}\right|

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(iv)}}{\gtrsim}\frac{\sigma^{2}n}{\lambda_{1}^{\star 2}}\left|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\star}\right|.

Here, (i) is valid since
\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\star}\cos\theta<|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{\perp}|\sin\theta
; (ii) holds given that
\max|a\pm b|\geq|b|
for any
a,b
; (iii) follows since, according to (
5.36
),
\cos\theta\asymp 1
holds with high probability;
and (iv) arises from the assumption (
3.3
).

Combining the preceding two cases, we can readily conclude that

\displaystyle\mathsf{dist}\left(u_{\bm{a}}^{\mathsf{plugin}},\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\star}\right)\gtrsim\frac{\sigma^{2}n}{\lambda_{1}^{\star 2}}\left|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\star}\right|

with probability at least
1/3
.

Appendix G
Technical lemmas

This section collects a few technical lemmas that prove useful in
the analysis of our main results. In what follows, we shall start
by stating the precise statements of these lemmas, followed by the
proofs for each of them.

Lemma 18
.

Let

\{\bm{h}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be a sequence of independent zero-mean
Gaussian random vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{r}
with covariance matrix

\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{r}
, and let
\bm{a}=[a_{i}]_{1\leq i\leq n}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

be a fixed vector. Then with probability at least
1-O\left(n^{-10}\right)
,
one has

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}a_{i}\big(\bm{h}_{i}\bm{h}_{i}^{\top}-\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{r}\big)\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sigma^{2}\big(\|\bm{a}\|_{2}\sqrt{r\log n}+\|\bm{a}\|_{\infty}(r\log n+\log^{2}n)\big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\sigma^{2}\|\bm{a}\|_{\infty}\big(\sqrt{rn\log n}+r\log n\big)

(G.1)

for some sufficiently large constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
. Here,
\|\bm{a}\|_{\infty}:=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}|a_{i}|
.

Lemma 19
.

Let
\{\bm{h}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}

and
\{\bm{g}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be two independent sequences of standard
Gaussian random vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{r}
and
\mathbb{R}^{p}
,
respectively. Then with probability at least
1-O\left(n^{-10}\right)
,
the following holds:

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}\bm{h}_{i}\bm{g}_{i}^{\top}\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\big(\sqrt{pn\log n}+\sqrt{pr}\log n\big)

where
C_{3}>0
is some sufficiently large constant.

Lemma 20
.

Let
\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be
a sequence of independent random variables in
\mathbb{R}
, and let

\mathcal{I}
be an interval in
\mathbb{R}
. Consider a collection
of functions
\{f_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
from
\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{I}

to
\mathbb{R}
, and we suppose that

1.

for any fixed
\lambda\in\mathcal{I}
, with probability at least

1-\delta_{1}
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}f_{i}(X_{i},\lambda)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2};

2.

with probability at least
1-\delta_{2}
,

\sup_{\lambda\in\mathcal{I}}\bigg|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\lambda}\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}f_{i}(X_{i},\lambda)\bigg|\leq L.

Then with probability exceeding
1-\frac{8L|\mathcal{I}|}{\varepsilon}\delta_{1}-\delta_{2}
,
one has

\sup_{\lambda\in\mathcal{I}}\bigg|\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}f_{i}(X_{i},\lambda)\bigg|\leq\varepsilon.

Next, we record an eigenvalue interlacing lemma, which has been documented
in
Horn and Johnson 2012
.

Lemma 21

(Poincaré separation theorem)
.

Let

\bm{M}
be a symmetric matrix in
\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
and
\bm{U}

be an orthonormal matrix in
\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}
satisfying
\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{U}=\bm{I}_{r}
.
Then one has

\lambda_{n-r+i}(\bm{M})\leq\lambda_{i}(\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{M}\bm{U})\leq\lambda_{i}(\bm{M}),\qquad 1\leq i\leq r,

where
\lambda_{i}(\bm{A})
denote the
i
-th largest eigenvalue
of matrix
\bm{A}
.

G.1
Proof of Lemma
18

As can be easily seen, the second inequality in (
G.1
)
follows immediately from the elementary bound
\|\bm{a}\|_{2}\leq\|\bm{a}\|_{\infty}\sqrt{n}
.
Hence, the proof boils down to justifying the first inequality in
(
G.1
).

We shall invoke the truncated matrix Bernstein inequality
(
Hopkins et al. 2016
, Proposition A.7)

to control the spectral norm of
\sum_{i}a_{i}\big(\bm{h}_{i}\bm{h}_{i}^{\top}-\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{r}\big)
,
which is a sum of independent zero-mean random matrices. To do so,
we need to bound three quantities: (1) the covariance of the sum
\sum_{i}a_{i}\big(\bm{h}_{i}\bm{h}_{i}^{\top}-\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{r}\big)
,
(2) a high-probability upper bound
L
on
\max_{i}\big\|a_{i}\big(\bm{h}_{i}\bm{h}_{i}^{\top}-\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{r}\big)\big\|
,
(3) the expectation of the truncated summand
\max_{i}\mathbb{E}\big[\|a_{i}\big(\bm{h}_{i}\bm{h}_{i}^{\top}-\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{r}\big)\|\ind\{\|a_{i}\big(\bm{h}_{i}\bm{h}_{i}^{\top}-\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{r}\big)\|\geq L\}\big]
.
We shall look at each of them separately.

1.

Straightforward computation gives

\bm{\Sigma}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[(\bm{h}_{i}\bm{h}_{i}^{\top}-\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{r})^{2}\big]=(r+1)\sigma^{4}\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}\bm{I}_{r}=(r+1)\sigma^{4}\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{2}\bm{I}_{r}.

(G.2)

2.

We now turn to bounding the spectral norm of each summand
a_{i}\big(\bm{h}_{i}\bm{h}_{i}^{\top}-\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{r}\big)
,
which clearly satisfies

\|a_{i}(\bm{h}_{i}\bm{h}_{i}^{\top}-\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{r})\|\leq|a_{i}|\cdot(\|\bm{h}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}+\sigma^{2}).

By virtue of the Gaussian concentration inequality
(
Hsu et al. 2012
, Proposition 1.1)
,
we obtain

\mathbb{P}\big\{\|\bm{h}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\sigma^{2}r\geq t\big\}\leq\exp\left(-\frac{1}{16}\min\Big\{\frac{t^{2}}{r\sigma^{4}},\,\frac{t}{\sigma^{2}}\Big\}\right).

(G.3)

In particular, this implies that with probability at least
1-O\left(n^{-20}\right)
,
one has

\|\bm{h}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\sigma^{2}\big(r+\log n\big).

(G.4)

In what follows, we shall set

L:=C\sigma^{2}\big(r+\log n\big)

(G.5)

for some sufficiently large constant
C>0
.

3.

We then look the truncated mean. To this end, we observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\|\bm{h}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\ind\{\|\bm{h}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\geq L\}\big]

\displaystyle\leq L\mathbb{P}\big\{\|\bm{h}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\geq L\big\}+\int_{L}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\big\{\|\bm{h}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\geq t\big\}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq O\left(n^{-20}\right)L+\int_{L}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\big\{\|\bm{h}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\geq t\big\}\,\mathrm{d}t.

For any
t\geq L/2
, it is seen that
\min\left\{t^{2}/(r\sigma^{4}),\,t/\sigma^{2}\right\}\geq t/\sigma^{2}
,
and hence

\displaystyle\int_{L}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\big\{\|\bm{h}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\geq t\big\}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{L/2}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\big\{\|\bm{h}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\sigma^{2}r\geq t\big\}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\int_{L/2}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{t}{16\sigma^{2}}\right)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{2}\exp\left(-\frac{C(r+\log n)}{32}\right)\lesssim\frac{L}{n^{2}},

provided that
C>0
is sufficiently large. As a result, taking this
together with
\|\bm{h}_{i}\bm{h}_{i}^{\top}-\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{r}\|\leq\|\bm{h}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}+\sigma^{2}
,
we arrive at

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\big[\|\bm{h}_{i}\bm{h}_{i}^{\top}-\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{r}\|\ind\{\|\bm{h}_{i}\bm{h}_{i}^{\top}-\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{r}\|\geq L\}\big]\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\big(\|\bm{h}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}+\sigma^{2}\big)\ind\{\|\bm{h}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}+\sigma^{2}\geq L\}\big]\lesssim\frac{L}{n^{2}}.

(G.6)

With the preceding bounds in place, we can invoke the truncated matrix
Bernstein inequality
(
Hopkins et al. 2016
, Proposition A.7)
to obtain
that: with probability at least
1-O\left(n^{-11}\right)
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{i}a_{i}\big(\bm{h}_{i}\bm{h}_{i}^{\top}-\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{r}\big)\Big\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\|\bm{\Sigma}\|\log n}+\|\bm{a}\|_{\infty}(nR+L\log n)

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{\asymp}\sqrt{\|\bm{\Sigma}\|\log n}+\|\bm{a}\|_{\infty}L\log n

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\lesssim}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}(r+1)\sigma^{4}\log n}+\|\bm{a}\|_{\infty}\sigma^{2}(r\log n+\log^{2}n)

\displaystyle\overset{}{\lesssim}\sigma^{2}\big(\|\bm{a}\|_{2}\sqrt{r\log n}+\|\bm{a}\|_{\infty}(r\log n+\log^{2}n)\big),

where (i) arises from (
G.6
),
and (ii) relies on (
G.2
)
and (
G.5
). This completes
the proof.

G.2
Proof of Lemma
19

The proof strategy here is almost identical to that for Lemma
18

— we shall apply the truncated matrix Bernstein inequality
(
Hopkins et al. 2016
, Proposition A.7)

to upper bound the spectral norm of
\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}\bm{h}_{i}\bm{g}_{i}^{\top}
,
which is a sum of independent zero-mean random matrices. Towards this,
we start by estimating several key quantities.

•

In view of the independence between
\{\bm{h}_{i}\}_{i}
and
\{\bm{g}_{i}\}_{i}
,
the covariance matrices can be computed as

\displaystyle\bm{\Sigma}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{h}_{i}\bm{g}_{i}^{\top}\bm{g}_{i}\bm{h}_{i}^{\top}\big]=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\bm{g}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{h}_{i}\bm{h}_{i}^{\top}\big]=np\bm{I}_{r};

(G.7)

\displaystyle\bm{\Sigma}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{g}_{i}\bm{h}_{i}^{\top}\bm{h}_{i}\bm{g}_{i}^{\top}\big]=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\bm{h}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{g}_{i}\bm{g}_{i}^{\top}\big]=nr\bm{I}_{p}.

(G.8)

•

As for the spectral norm of each summand
\bm{h}_{i}\bm{g}_{i}^{\top}
,
we know from (
G.4
) that with probability
at least
1-O\left(n^{-20}\right)
,

\|\bm{h}_{i}\bm{g}_{i}^{\top}\|=\|\bm{h}_{i}\|_{2}\|\bm{g}_{i}\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{(r+\log n)(p+\log n)}\asymp\sqrt{pr}+\sqrt{p\log n}+\log n.

Therefore, this suggests that we define

L:=C\big(\sqrt{pr}+\sqrt{p\log n}+\log n\big)

(G.9)

for some sufficiently large constant
C>0
.

•

Next, we turn to the truncated mean. Observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\|\bm{h}_{i}\bm{g}_{i}^{\top}\|\ind\{\|\bm{h}_{i}\bm{g}_{i}^{\top}\|\geq L\}\big]

\displaystyle\leq L\mathbb{P}\big\{\|\bm{h}_{i}\bm{g}_{i}^{\top}\|\geq L\big\}+\int_{L}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\big\{\|\bm{h}_{i}\bm{g}_{i}^{\top}\|\geq t\big\}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq O\left(n^{-20}\right)L+\int_{L}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\big\{\|\bm{h}_{i}\bm{g}_{i}^{\top}\|\geq t\big\}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq O\left(n^{-20}\right)L+\int_{L}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\big\{\|\bm{h}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\geq t\big\}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{L}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\big\{\|\bm{g}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\geq t\big\}\,\mathrm{d}t,

where the last holds arises from the following bound due to the union
bound:

\mathbb{P}\big\{\|\bm{h}_{i}\bm{g}_{i}^{\top}\|\geq t\big\}=\mathbb{P}\big\{\|\bm{h}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{g}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\geq t^{2}\big\}\leq\mathbb{P}\big\{\|\bm{h}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\geq t\big\}+\mathbb{P}\big\{\|\bm{g}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\geq t\big\}.

In addition, since
\min\left\{t^{2}/r,\,t\right\}\geq t
for all

t\geq L/2\geq 2r
, we can use (
G.3
) to
bound

\displaystyle\int_{L}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\big\{\|\bm{h}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\geq t\big\}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{L}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big\{\|\bm{h}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-r\geq\frac{t}{2}\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\int_{L/2}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\big\{\|\bm{h}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-r\geq t\big\}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{L/2}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{16}\min\Big\{\frac{t^{2}}{r},\,t\Big\}\right)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\int_{L/2}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{t}{16}\right)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp\left(-\frac{C}{32}\sqrt{pr+p\log n+\log^{2}n}\right)\lesssim\frac{L}{n^{2}}.

Clearly, the same bound also holds for
\int_{L}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\big\{\|\bm{g}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\geq t\big\}\,\mathrm{d}t
.
Therefore, combining these estimates yields

R:=\mathbb{E}\big[\|\bm{h}_{i}\bm{g}_{i}^{\top}\|\ind\{\|\bm{h}_{i}\bm{g}_{i}^{\top}\|\geq L\}\big]\lesssim\frac{L}{n^{2}}.

(G.10)

With these parameters in place, one can apply the truncated matrix
Bernstein inequality
(
Hopkins et al. 2016
, Proposition A.7)
to demonstrate
that: with probability at least
1-O\left(n^{-10}\right)
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}\bm{h}_{i}\bm{g}_{i}^{\top}\Big\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{(\|\bm{\Sigma}_{1}\|+\|\bm{\Sigma}_{2}\|)\log n}+nR+L\log n

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{\asymp}\sqrt{\|\bm{\Sigma}_{1}\|\log n}+L\log n

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\lesssim}\sqrt{pn\log n}+\sqrt{pr}\log n+\sqrt{p\log^{3}n}+\log^{2}n

\displaystyle\asymp\sqrt{pn\log n}+\sqrt{pr}\log n.

Here, (i) uses (
G.7
), (
G.7
)
and (
G.10
); (ii) arises from (
G.9
).
The proof is thus complete.

G.3
Proof of Lemma
20

Let
\mathcal{N}
be a
\frac{\varepsilon}{2L}
-covering of
\mathcal{I}

with cardinality
|\mathcal{N}|\leq\frac{4L|\mathcal{I}|}{\varepsilon}
,
and let
\mathcal{E}
denote an event such that

\displaystyle\sup_{\lambda\in\mathcal{N}}\bigg|\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}f_{i}(X_{i},\lambda)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2},

\displaystyle\sup_{\lambda\in\mathcal{I}}\bigg|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\lambda}\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}f_{i}(X_{i},\lambda)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq L,

which holds with probability at least
1-\frac{8L|\mathcal{I}|}{\varepsilon}\delta_{1}-\delta_{2}

(according to the assumptions and the union bound).

For any
\lambda\in\mathcal{I}
, let
\hat{\lambda}\in\mathcal{N}

such that
|\lambda-\hat{\lambda}|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2L}
. One
can easily check that on the event
\mathcal{E}
, one has

\displaystyle\sup_{\lambda\in\mathcal{I}}\bigg|\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}f_{i}(X_{i},\lambda)\bigg|

\displaystyle=\sup_{\lambda\in\mathcal{I}}\bigg|\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}\big(f_{i}(X_{i},\lambda)-f_{i}(X_{i},\hat{\lambda})+f_{i}(X_{i},\hat{\lambda})\big)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\lambda\in\mathcal{I}}\bigg|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\lambda}\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}f_{i}(X_{i},\lambda)\bigg|\cdot|\lambda-\hat{\lambda}|+\sup_{\hat{\lambda}\in\mathcal{N}}\bigg|\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}f_{i}(X_{i},\hat{\lambda})\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq L\cdot\frac{\varepsilon}{2L}+\sup_{\hat{\lambda}\in\mathcal{N}}\bigg|\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}f_{i}(X_{i},\hat{\lambda})\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}=\varepsilon,

thus concluding the proof.
